| `mg1` | `fixed tbb8:T = 100tbb8 + 100tbb8;` | `i8 200` (−56; the run time: ERR) | NITPICK-TYPE-035 |
| `mg2` | `fixed tfp64:Q = 2tfp64 + 1tfp64;` | `i64 3` | NITPICK-TYPE-035 |
| `mh2` | `fixed tryte:TR = 29524 + 1;` | `i16 29525` (the run time: ERR) | NITPICK-TYPE-035 |
| `mk2` | `tfp64:x = 0Tttfp64;` (a body) | 0 for −1 | −4294967296 |
| `mn2` | `pick (q) { (2tfp64) { … } … }`, `q = 2tfp64` | the wildcard arm (exit 1) | the arm (exit 0) |
| `mn1` | `(1tfp64..2tfp64)` over 1.5 | the wildcard arm | the arm |
| `mi1` | `int8:x = comptime(100 + 100);` | `store i8 200` | NITPICK-TYPE-031 |
| `mi3` | `int32:x = comptime(BIG);`, `BIG` an `int64` 5000000000 | `store i32 5000000000` | NITPICK-TYPE-007 |
| `mf4b` | `flt64:l = 3f64;` | `llc`: "integer constant must have integer type" | `double 3.0` |
| `mk1` | `flt32:x = 100000000f32;` | the checker traps (exit 3) | compiles |
| `mf5c` | `fixed int32?:O = NIL;` | NITPICK-TYPE-004 (the bindings analysis) | `zeroinitializer` |
| `mj2` | `int8[4294967298]:a = [1i8, 2i8];` | an array of two | NITPICK-TYPE-004 |

A function body computed the twisted arithmetic correctly (`mh1`: exit 0 on both) — the emitter's constant shortcut
asks for a plain integer type; only the module binding's renderer and `comptime` asked nothing.

**The design.** ONE RULE: the folder computes in the plain integers (and a flag family's bits) and carries every other
numeric family. `fold_suffixed_literal` gives a literal its suffix's family — `CV_INT` at the `tbb` type, `CV_CHAR`
for a `charN` suffix, `CV_REAL` (new: the literal's decimal text, a sign, the type or none) for a float, a `tfp`, a
`dim256` and any fraction; `fold_computes` gates every operator arm; a minus on a carried constant flips its sign
(exact: a float's sign bit, a symmetric range). `fold_stamp` gives a folded NAME its binding's declared type, bounded
(`stamp_depth`) against a declared type that names its own binding. `const_init_verdict` (type_stmt.npk) is the one
gate: 0 a constant, 1 not, 2 an expression of a carried family ("written, not computed"), 3 a `flt32`; it reads the
family off the type the checker recorded, so an unsuffixed ternary literal under arithmetic is caught, and a member
whose type was already refused adds no second report (D-240). `const_scalar_text` (ir_expr.npk) renders by that type;
`emit_int` lowers the integer-form float; the `comptime` arm renders a carried constant. `pat_q_text` (ir_stmt.npk) spells a
fixed-point selector's value pattern and range bounds as Q constants (DEF-204). The bindings analysis's
re-fold is deleted. Three type-level sites ask a plain integer in range before narrowing.

**What the review of my own change found** (each probed): the stamping recursed without bound on `fixed int32[K]:K =
5;` — each `fold_const` starts with fresh fuel — and trapped the checker (`ml1`); bounded. `tid` is a kernel-identifier
type keyword (a parse error two lines from the cause). A `comptime` that did not fold reported TYPE-004 and then
TYPE-035 (two reports): the verdict is silent for a member whose type was refused.

**Tests.** `tests/backend/programs/module_constants.npk` (sixty cases; exit 0 at both legs; `f13914c`'s checker traps
on it) and `pick_fixed_point.npk` (fourteen cases; exit 0 at both legs; EMIT-002 on `f13914c`);
`tests/types/rejection/module_const_carried.npk` (NITPICK-TYPE-035 ×10, eight controls) and
`comptime_width.npk` (NITPICK-TYPE-031 ×3, TYPE-007 ×2, TYPE-004 ×5, TYPE-001 ×1, six controls), headers measured to a fixed point and read against their lines.

**Measured.** The sweep under `f13914c`'s checker and this one over 3,605 files: 6 sites
vanished, 17 appeared, every one in this landing's own three test files (the base checker's trap on `module_constants.npk` gone; the two rejection files' sites, six of them reported by the base checker at other lines or under other codes) — no other unit of the tree and no file of the libraries or the applications moves; per code TYPE-001 49 → 50, TYPE-004 29 → 31, TYPE-007 150 → 151, TYPE-031 31 → 34, TYPE-035 7 → 12, TRAP 1 → 0 (2,408 → 2,419
sites). The emission comparison: 540 programs, 538 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 2 refused by `f13914c`'s alone (the two new programs). The libraries'
and applications' programs under both compilers: 1,562 identical, 1,030 refused by both, 0 different,
0 newly refused. D-332's count: 0 mismatched files. The manifest: `npkg verify --record` — 6,855 obligations
(3,312 discharged, 2,651 open, 0 budget, 887 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler
rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 unmoved; `nitpick.obligations` 6,553 rows before and
6,560 after, 6,291 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows; the four (symbol, kind) pairs that read as fallen are a MOVE (`is_const_initialiser`'s div-min, div-zero, overflow and terminate rows are `const_init_verdict`'s, verdict for verdict). Every file of `tests/verify` decided by hand with this compiler: 128 of 128 pass. The frontend's 32 and the emitter's 5 unit
tests pass; the in-process checks 0 failures.

**Registered with it.** DEF-202 (a reference to an aggregate binding is not a constant), DEF-203 (a `flt32` literal's
two roundings; the next landing), DEF-205 (a float literal past its type's range is infinity, one below it zero, in
silence: probe `ms1`), S-125 (may the compiler compute in the carried families; recommended: no) and S-126 (refuse a
float literal that rounds to infinity or to zero; lift the 15-digit rule after DEF-203). The advance notice was F29.

### DEF-203, a `flt32` literal in one rounding — LANDED 2026-10-01 (`nitpick-compiler_26`; worktree `wt/26a`, branch `26-def203` on `789ffdc`; landing 88)

**Measured first, on `789ffdc`.** (1) THE ROADS. DEF-203 was registered for a literal in a body. Every road a
`flt32` constant takes gave the same two-rounding float for `9.51125303839185e-19` (`0x218C5C80`, the nearest
`0x218C5C7F`): under a sign, with no suffix in a `flt32` slot, through `comptime(…)`, as a struct field, an array
element, a `simd` lane and splat, a `complex` component (probes `fa1`…`fa7` in `wt/26a/.internal/probes`); the integer
form is exact through a double (`16777217f32` is the tie, and went to even); negative zero kept its sign. One defect,
three writers. (2) THE OTHER HALF OF D-143. "LLVM's parser converts decimal to double with correct rounding" had never
been measured. A first rig held it on 10,015 adversarial texts (as `double <text>` through `llc`, the object's bytes
against exact rationals: exact midpoints between doubles written out to some 770 digits and the texts one unit in the
last place beside them, subnormal midpoints, the largest double and half-way to infinity, every power of ten), and on
that the landing was scoped to `flt32`. THE COMMITTED MEASUREMENT (`float_vectors.py --llvm`), run after the source was
final, FOUND WHERE IT FAILS: the two vectors written for `float_round`'s own exponent bound — a fraction with a hundred
thousand leading zeros and the exponent that undoes them — are zero to LLVM. Its parser caps the exponent it reads at
24,000: `0.<24000 zeros>1e24001`, the number 1.0, is the double 0.1 (DEF-209, registered: the next landing). Within
that bound: 8,890 texts, none different. (3) TWO HOLES BESIDE IT (DEF-206):
an unsuffixed fraction takes a float slot's width through `lit_ranged`, which holds no float rule — a nineteen-digit
fraction in a `flt32` slot was accepted, and `flt128:x = 1.0;` passed the checker and died as EMIT-002; `comptime(…)`
of an unsuffixed fraction was a third road with the same hole, found by probing every position after the first fix.

**The design.** `float_round(text, width)` in `numeric.npk`, generic in the format (24 bits and 127; 53 and 1023), with
no wide integer: the literal's digits go into one run with the point's place counted; the integer part is halved until
nothing is left, its bits arriving least significant first into a window of p + 1 bits (the significand and the round
bit) with a sticky bit for what falls out below; the fraction is doubled, its bits arriving most significant first,
until the window is full (after the first 1, for a number below one; fewer bits for a subnormal); what remains of the
fraction is sticky; one rounding to nearest, ties to even; the encoding `((max(e, emin) − emin) << (p − 1)) + m`
absorbs a carry out of the significand, into the next exponent or into infinity's own pattern. A decimal exponent past
310 or below −330 is answered without work. The answer carries `inf` and `zero` (a NONZERO number rounding to zero)
and decides neither: DEF-205, S-126. The algorithm was first written as a Python model and held to `fractions.Fraction`
on 57,552 texts, then written in Nitpick and held to the same reference on 42,501 texts at both widths through a dump
tool, before any caller existed: no mismatch in either.

**The change.** The emitter: `float_const_text(text, neg, bits)` is the ONE writer of a float constant — `double <body>`
for a `flt64`, and for a `flt32` `0x` and the sixteen hex digits of the double holding the float (`flt32_bits_as_flt64`,
`hex64_text`) — behind `emit_float_const` (a literal, the integer form with its sign carried apart, a `comptime` value)
and `const_scalar_text` (a module binding's initialiser); the three `fptrunc double … to float` writers are gone.
`const_init_verdict` loses its third verdict. The encoder: `fp_literal` is `((_ to_fp eb sb) RNE <the exact real>)` at
the literal's own format — it had mirrored the two roundings on purpose, so it changed in the same commit. The checker:
`float_text_ok`, D-143's two literal rules in one helper asked of the text, by the suffix's branch, the contextual one
and `type_comptime`.

**What asking how it fails found** (each probed). The exponent's bound was first `tfp_q_decimal`'s — the digits stop
at 100,000 — and that is WRONG for a legal text: a fraction with a hundred thousand leading zeros and the exponent that
undoes them read as a millionth of itself. The exponent is bounded by the text's own length now (past it and a
thousand more, no digit can bring the number back), and two such texts are vectors. The same question asked of the
encoder found DEF-207: `decimal_to_real` had no bound at all, so `1.0e999999f64` under a row did not finish and a
twenty-digit exponent trapped the compiler, in a build that writes obligations; a number past 10^400 or below 10^-400
is written as 10^400 or 10^-400 now, which is the same float and the same twin. `tfp_q_decimal` itself refuses such a
text rather than misreading it (DEF-208, registered).

**What followed from it, read before it was kept.** A `flt32` literal had been a `narrow` definition to the encoder,
and tier 2's bound test (`t2_bounds_in`) takes only a `lit` for a literal: no `flt32` row was ever eligible for the
Real-interval twin. They are now. The twin's text for `flt32` was read (`eps = 1/16777216`, `eta = 2^−149`, every
magnitude under `(2^24 − 1)·2^104`), and is the model D-281 gives; `flt32_round.npk` pins the first such discharge.

**Tests.** `tests/frontend/float_round.npk` (2,072 vectors and 75 bit patterns, from exact rationals;
generated). `tests/backend/programs/float_literal_kat.npk` (505 `flt32` and 500 `flt64` literals read back
as their bits at both legs; generated; `789ffdc` exits 12: 44 of its `flt32` literals are the wrong float
there). `tests/backend/programs/flt32_literal.npk` (every road, on four fifteen-digit counterexamples; a rule's and a
contract's bound; fifteen module-constant forms; `789ffdc` refuses it for the module constants). `tests/verify/
flt32_round.npk` (`prove` over the literal: refuted on `789ffdc`, discharged; and the `flt32` tier-2 row: `budget`
there, discharged, tier `real`). `tests/types/rejection/float_rules.npk` (TYPE-030 ×8: four new sites — an unsuffixed fraction and a `comptime` of
one, in a `flt32` and in a `flt128` slot — and two controls; `789ffdc` reports four of the eight)
and `module_const_carried.npk` (its `flt32` site is a computed one now; two written `flt32` controls, which `789ffdc`
refuses; the unsuffixed and `comptime` sites). `bootstrap/generator/float_vectors.py` writes the two generated files, and the harness's
`check_generated_current` holds them current.

**Measured.** The sweep under `789ffdc`'s checker and this one over 3,611 files: 14 sites
vanished and 4 appeared — the 14 are NITPICK-TYPE-035 at the `flt32` module constants of the landing's own two test files (twelve in `flt32_literal.npk`, the two written controls of `module_const_carried.npk`) and the 4 are the new NITPICK-TYPE-030 sites of `float_rules.npk`; no file of the libraries, the applications or the fuzzer's corpus moves, and none of the tree's own beyond those three; per code TYPE-030 10 → 14, TYPE-035 26 → 12 (2,433 → 2,423
sites). The emission over 543 programs of the tree: 535 byte-identical, 7 different, 0 newly refused, 1 refused by `789ffdc` alone (`flt32_literal.npk`, for its module constants). The five
existing programs whose text differs are THE SAME PROGRAM through their constants: each base emission with its
`fptrunc double <text> to float` lines read as the float those two roundings give, each new one with its `float 0x…`
read as its bits, temporaries renumbered — identical line for line (`float.npk`, `cast_wide.npk`, `cast_range_edges.npk`, `float_literal_forms.npk`, `module_constants.npk`; the other two that differ are this landing's own `float_literal_kat.npk`, by exactly the 44 literals whose float moved, and `flt32_round.npk`, by its one). The row texts of `tests/verify/`
under both compilers: 128 of 129 files byte-identical, the 129th this landing's own `flt32_round.npk`. The
programs' exits at both legs under this compiler: 364 programs, 355 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The libraries and applications, 2,594 programs:
1,560 byte-identical, 1,030 refused by both, 4 different (in text only: the fuzzer's `m10/programs/f02_flt32_rounds_in_flt32.npk` and `m11/programs/ty0017.npk`, `ty0189.npk`, `ty0190b.npk`, each the same program through its `flt32` constants; 0 newly refused). D-332's count: 0 mismatched
files. The frontend's 33 and the emitter's 5 unit tests pass. `tests/verify/`: 129 of 129 pass. The
manifest: 6,985 obligations (3,383 discharged, 2,700 open, 0 budget, 897 unencoded, 5 checker), matching,
the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 unmoved; `nitpick.obligations`
6,560 rows before and 6,690 after, 6,453 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows,
the four (symbol, kind) pairs that read as fallen are two MOVED bodies, verdict for verdict (`pow2_double_signed`'s hex loop is `hex64_text`'s; `type_numeric_literal`'s digit count is `float_text_ok`'s and `float_literal_ok`'s). The in-process checks: 0 failures.

**Registered with it.** DEF-209 (a `flt64` literal's bits are still LLVM's parse of its text, which caps the exponent
at 24,000: a silent wrong double for a literal of some 24 KB; the next landing writes a `flt64`'s bits from the
compiler's conversion, the proof by reading both emissions' constants through). DEF-208 (`tfp_q_decimal`'s
exponent test). DEF-205 and S-126 stand as put; the flags they need exist.

### DEF-209, a `flt64` literal by its bits — LANDED 2026-10-01 (`nitpick-compiler_27`; worktree `wt/27a`, branch `27-def209` on `88aa21d`; landing 89)

**Measured first, on `88aa21d`, through the whole compiler.** DEF-209 was registered from `llc` alone
(`float_vectors.py --llvm`). Five generated programs, each one `flt64` literal read back as its bits, at both legs:
`0.<N zeros>1e<N+1>f64` — the number 1.0 — is 1.0 at N = 23,990, the double 0.1 at N = 24,000, zero at N = 30,000
and at N = 100,005; `1<24001 zeros>.0e-24001f64` is 10.0. The 100 KB literal costs 0.45 s from source to exit code
at both legs: nothing in the lexer, the interner, the checker or the emitter is nonlinear in a literal's length. And
the verified build's face: `prove(x == 1.0f64)` over such a literal is DISCHARGED (the solver's term is `to_fp RNE`
of the exact real) while the binary holds 0.1 — the verified binary of `88aa21d` exits 1.

**The change.** `float_const_text(text, neg, bits)` (ir_expr.npk) is one branch for both widths: `float_round(text,
bits)`, a `flt32`'s float widened to the double that holds it, the sign bit, `0x` and sixteen hex digits.
`float_literal_body` — the reader that cut the suffix, completed a pointless `1e10` and handed the decimal to LLVM —
is deleted, and the writer no longer takes the emitter. Three comments that described the old road are corrected
(ir_expr.npk, numeric.npk, smt_encode.npk: the encoder's own header still said a `flt32` literal was spelled as two
roundings). No other source line moves: the encoder's `fp_literal` was already the exact real at the literal's
format.

**Asked how it could fail, before it was kept** (each read or probed). (1) TWO READERS OF ONE TEXT:
`float_literal_body` cut at the first `f` and LLVM read the rest; `float_round` reads digits, one point, an exponent
and its sign and stops at the first other byte. On a legal text (LEXICAL_REFERENCE §6.2; LEX-009 holds the tail to a
`TypeSuffix`) they are the same number, and the emission comparison below is the measurement. (2) A SITE THAT READS
A CONSTANT'S TEXT: none — no character of a value's reference is read anywhere in the emitter; a negated literal is
an `fneg` instruction on the constant; the one place a sign is put on text is the writer. (3) A SECOND WRITER: what
the backend writes as a decimal beside `double` is its own exact bounds (`cast_bounds.npk`'s `-1.0`, `-129.0`,
`-32769.0`, `-2147483649.0`, a ternary family's bound plus one, one `0.0`), small integers LLVM cannot misread and
no program's text; they stay. (4) EVERY ROAD: `flt32_literal.npk` re-spelled at `flt64` holds 47 decimal constants
(17 distinct) under `88aa21d` and none under this compiler; the two emissions are one artifact. (5) A TEST THAT
CANNOT SEE THE DEFECT: a short literal is read alike by LLVM's parser and by `float_round`, so a road test with
short texts is blind to a writer that bypasses the conversion — each call site of the writer gets a text past the
cap (below).

**The proof that no other constant moved.** Every program with a float literal changes its emitted text, so "the
emission byte-identical" cannot be the measurement. Each differing pair of the two emission comparisons — the tree's
34 of 544 programs and the libraries' and applications' 31 of 2,602 — was put through two legs that share nothing.
LEG B, THE TEXTS (`.internal/tools/fnorm2.py`): every floating-point constant on both sides becomes one token, the
bits of a double — a decimal through exact rationals (`fractions.Fraction`, the vector generator's `ref`), `0x` and
sixteen hex digits as itself — and the two texts must then be byte-identical: 65 of 65. It says the new spelling is
the number written and nothing else moved. LEG A, THE ARTIFACTS (`objcmp.sh`): both emissions assembled under ONE
module name (an emission states no `source_filename`, so `llc` names the module by its input file and the name
reaches the object: the same text under two names is two objects, measured) with the manifest's flags — the `-O0`
object, the `opt -O2` text and the `-O2` object byte-identical: 63 of 65. It says LLVM read the old text as the bits
the new text states. The 2 pairs where leg A differs are this landing's tests, which hold a literal past the cap:
there the old artifact is the wrong one, and leg B still holds. And the corpus itself as the measurement of LLVM's
parser: every distinct decimal the old emissions held within the cap — 593 texts, the longest 115 characters, the
largest exponent 324 — as `double <text>` through the pinned `llc` against the exact rationals: none read as another
double (the 5 past the cap, all in this landing's tests, each read as another: the defect). And `float_round`
itself, the one authority at both widths now, was run against the exact rationals on 566,680 adversarial texts at
both formats (the dev tool `frdump`, forty seeds of every family): no mismatch.

**Tests.** `bootstrap/generator/float_vectors.py`: `PAST_CAP`, four texts — the number 1.0 written past the cap from
both sides, and an exponent that undoes a hundred thousand leading zeros (1.0 and 5.0) — read by the unit vectors at
both widths (`tests/frontend/float_round.npk`: 2,076 vectors) and by the compiled half at `flt64`
(`tests/backend/programs/float_literal_kat.npk`: 505 `flt32` and 504 `flt64` literals), which also gains `roads`: a
module binding, a negated one, an aggregate's element, a literal with no suffix in a `flt64` slot, `comptime(…)` and
`comptime(-…)`, each with a text past the cap. Under `88aa21d` the file exits 26 (the `w` function that holds the
four texts), and a probe that masks the six roads answers 63: every road fails alone.
`tests/verify/flt64_past_cap.npk`: two `prove(x == 1.0f64)` rows over the two 24,001 texts — discharged under both
compilers; the verified binary exits 0 and `88aa21d`'s exits 1. A generated `uint64` past 2^63 (a negative double's
bits) is built with `| (1u64 << 63u64)`: LEX-004 otherwise (D-311).

**Measured.** The sweep under `88aa21d`'s checker and this one over 3,612 files: 0 sites vanished, 0 appeared (no
rule changed). The emission: 544 programs, 510 byte-identical, 34 different in text, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by
`88aa21d` alone; the libraries and applications 2,602 programs: 1,535 the same, 1,036 refused by both, 31 different
in text (all in the fuzzer's corpus) — every one through both legs above. D-332's count over the rejection suites: 0
mismatched files. The rows of `tests/verify/` under both compilers: 130 of 130 files byte-identical (the encoder is
untouched; the one new file exists on both sides). Every program at both legs: 364 programs, 355 at their expected
exits, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's 33 and the
emitter's 5 unit tests pass; `tests/verify/` 130 of 130 under the verified build. The manifest: 6,975 obligations
(3,375 discharged, 2,698 open, 0 budget, 897 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding
itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 unmoved; `nitpick.obligations` 6,690 rows before and 6,680 after, 6,586
shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows; the two (symbol, kind) pairs that read as fallen are
`float_literal_body`'s own (4 overflow and 4 terminate rows discharged), deleted with the function -- the manifest
is ten rows shorter, its ten.

**Registered with it.** DEF-210 (a refused `#[derive(Copy)]` over an array of non-scalars says `Clone`: a wording
defect in `dv_refusal`, found measuring the library seat's documentation note). That note is taken here:
TRAITS_REFERENCE said "all seven generate" and its DERIVE-006 list had no `Copy`; the section names the eight and
what `Copy` admits (a scalar, a pointer, a `simd` value, an array of scalars, a `Copy` named type) and refuses, each
measured on `88aa21d`. DEF-205 and S-126 stand as put: the two flags are the conversion's at both widths now and

### Step 3b — LANDED 2026-10-01 (`nitpick-compiler_28`; worktree `wt/28a`, branch `28-1e-3b` on `776383f`; landing 90; D-338; DEF-211 and DEF-213 fixed; DEF-212, DEF-214, S-127, S-128 registered; D-339…D-346 recorded)

**Measured first** (§2.10d): `mc0309b` NITPICK-TYPE-004 on `776383f`, with `?!` over a compile-time call, an enum
value and a `pick` in a `comptime` body each TYPE-004 beside it; the run time's `pick` and string order read off
probes at both legs (source order, a guard after its pattern, `fall` with no test, unsigned bytes). Three defects
found before the plan was written (DEF-211, DEF-212, DEF-213) and a fourth by the tests (DEF-214).

**What landed** (`src/frontend/type_resolve.npk`, with one arm each in `type_expr.npk` and `type_stmt.npk`).
`CV_ENUM` — the enum's type and the variant's position among its declaration's members; `fold_variant`
(`Enum.Variant` by the base identifier's recorded symbol; a generic enum's and a payload-carrying variant are not
values); the enum arm of `fold_binary` (`==`/`!=` where the checker's `equatable` admits one); `fold_string_cmp`
with `string_byte_order` (the prelude's `Ordering` found in the prelude's own scope); `?!` and `?|` folding through
their operand; `fold_pick` with `pick_selects`, `fold_arm_matches`, `fold_pat_matches`, `cv_matches` and
`cv_bound_agrees` — a pattern the checker never typed against its selector (DEF-178, open) DECLINES where its kind,
its written type or its range disagrees, and never answers "no match"; two new `FoldFlow` states, `gave` and
`fall_to`, which the block walk and both loop walkers propagate through one question (`flow_leaves`);
`TypeResolver.fold_frames`, raised and lowered by `fold_call` alone, read by the `pick` expression's arm and by
`fold_const` (DEF-213); the short-circuit in `fold_binary` (DEF-211); `type_comptime` refusing an enum value with
its own sentence; `const_init_verdict` answering 1 for one. No emitter, encoder or floor byte.

**Departures from the plan as first written, each measured.** (1) The twin tests could not call one `comptime func:`
at both times: the emitter emits none, and the call reaches `llc` undefined — DEF-214, on `776383f` and this tree
alike, with S-128 under it. Every test function is written twice from one template (`c_f`, `r_f`). (2) A binding arm
is not a refusal: its variant can never be a payload-less selector's, so it is passed over as at run time (`p_msg`);
the rejection file holds a generic enum's variant in its place. (3) No unit case was added to the frontend suites:
the two programs ask the evaluator and the machine the same ninety questions, which is the stronger instrument. (4)
My own test first expected `"z"` to order after `"\xC3\xA9"`; the evaluator, the machine and arithmetic said
otherwise, and the constant is `E_ACUTE_NOT_BEFORE_Z`.

**The measurements** (base = `776383f`'s binaries). The listener's `mc0309b`: 0/0 at both legs. **The sweep** under
both checkers over 3,624 files (the tree's units and every `.npk` of the library and application repositories): 107

### DEF-205 under D-346, a float literal fits its type — LANDED 2026-10-01 (`nitpick-compiler_27`; worktree `wt/27b`, branch `27-def205` on `eaf6b08`; landing 91)

**Measured first, on `eaf6b08`.** Eight roads a float literal takes — a body literal at both widths past the range
and below it, an unsuffixed one in a `flt32` slot, `comptime(…)`, a `flt32` and a `flt64` module constant — read
back as bits at both legs: eight silent infinities and zeros (probe `fz1`, exit 255), the checker silent.

**The change.** `float_fit_refusal(text, bits)` in `numeric.npk`, beside the conversion: "" where the number
written, rounded once to the type, is finite and is zero only where zero was written; else the sentence.
`float_text_ok` (type_expr.npk) asks it and loses the 15-digit count (D-346's two halves, in one function). The
message names the type and says what the literal would have been.

**Probed after it looked complete** (the standing lesson, and it paid again). Every position a float literal can sit
in — a struct field, an array element, a `simd` lane, a `complex` component, an operand of `+` and of `<`, an
argument, under a sign, a cast's operand, a `Rules` clause, a contract, a `comptime func:`'s body, a module binding,
an unsuffixed fraction handed through a `comptime func:` — one report each. And one road stood open: A LITERAL UNDER
A `comptime(…)` OPERAND IS TYPED BY NOBODY. `type_comptime` folds its operand and never types it, so
`comptime(1.0e999f64)`, `comptime(-1.0e39f32)`, a parenthesised or nested one, and a literal handed to a `comptime
func:` inside the operand were still infinity with the checker's rule standing — and the same road had carried a
sixteen-digit suffixed `flt32` literal past the 15-digit rule since landing 88 (DEF-206's fourth road; `fq2`). So
the evaluator asks the question where it reads a float literal (`fold_suffixed_literal`), OUTSIDE A CALL: inside one
the literal sits in a function's body, which the checker types, and a report with a call chain behind it would be a
second sentence about one literal. Outside a call it speaks the checker's own sentence at the literal's own span, so
a literal both read (a module binding's initialiser is typed and then folded) is one report whichever comes first.
`comptime(M)` and `comptime(raw f())` over a refused literal add nothing.

**The emitter holds the promise.** `float_const_text` returns no constant for a literal whose conversion reports
either flag; its callers confess (NITPICK-EMIT-002). No road is known to reach it — that is the point of a belt: a
text that arrives by a road nobody asked is a loud compiler defect, never an infinity written in silence. A `pick`
pattern over a float selector, the one place a float literal sits that neither reader types, is DEF-178's and is
EMIT-002 before and after.

**Tests.** `tests/types/rejection/float_fit.npk`: eighteen sites — the suffix, the slot, `comptime(…)` of a bare
fraction, three literals under a `comptime(…)` operand, under a sign, two module constants, and the four edges of
the two formats (`3.40282357e38f32`, `1.7976931348623159e308f64`, `7.0e-46f32`, `2.4703282292062327e-324f64`) — and
eleven controls on the other side of each edge (the largest values, the texts that round to them, the smallest
subnormals, the texts that round up to them, an exact zero with a huge exponent, a twenty-digit `flt32` literal),
each edge checked against exact rationals before it was written. `float_rules.npk`: `flt128`'s five sites; its three
digit sites are controls. `float_vectors.py`: a `flt32` literal of the compiled half runs to 25 digits and to exact
midpoints written out (535 `flt32` and 504 `flt64` literals).

**Measured.** The sweep under `eaf6b08`'s checker and this one over 3,625 files: 105 sites vanished, 18 appeared —
the 105 are NITPICK-TYPE-030 at long `flt32` literals -- 97 in the regenerated `float_literal_kat.npk` (its `flt32`
literals run past fifteen digits now), 3 in `float_rules.npk` and 2 in `float_fit.npk` (the digit sites, controls
now), and 3 in the fuzzer's corpus, one each in the three programs that expect the lifted rule
(`m10/programs/c25_flt32_literal_16_digits.npk`, `m11/programs/vf0983.npk`, `m11/programs/vf1149.npk`); the 18 are
NITPICK-TYPE-031 in `float_fit.npk`; no file of the libraries, the applications or the fuzzer's corpus writes a
float literal that does not fit. The emission: 546 programs, 545 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 1
refused by `eaf6b08` alone (`float_literal_kat.npk`, for its `flt32` literals past fifteen digits); the libraries
and applications 2,602 programs: 1,567 emit byte-identically, 1,032 are refused by both compilers, 3 are refused by
`eaf6b08` alone and compile now (the three fuzz programs above), 0 differ, 0 are newly refused. D-332's count: 0
mismatched files. The rows of `tests/verify/` under both compilers: 130 of 130 files byte-identical. Every program
at both legs: 366 programs, 357 at their expected exits, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the
harness's driver, by design. The frontend's 33 and the emitter's 5 unit tests pass; `tests/verify/` 130 of 130 under
the verified build. The manifest: 6,990 obligations (3,388 discharged, 2,700 open, 0 budget, 897 unencoded, 5
checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 unmoved;
`nitpick.obligations` 6,702 rows before and 6,695 after, 6,458 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows; the
two (symbol, kind) pairs that read as fallen are `float_text_ok`'s digit-count loop (2 overflow and 2 terminate rows
discharged), deleted with the rule.

### DEF-183 part B — LANDED 2026-10-01 (`nitpick-compiler_29`; worktree `wt/29a`, branch `29-def183b` on `c15422e`; landing 92; under D-340; DEF-220, DEF-221, DEF-222 and DEF-223 found by its probes and fixed)

**Measured first** (probes `.internal/probes/a*`, `b*`, `c*`, base = `eaf6b08`, both legs). `#twice(shared)` beside
a module `shared` answered 200 for 10 (the module's binding read); `#twice(n)` with a local `n` was RESOLVE-002; a
local the BODY had declared captured the argument (`int32:tmp = 1i32; ... tmp + X` over the caller's `tmp`: 2 for
8), and so did the body's own `for` binding (3 for 30); a declaration macro's argument was captured by a PARAMETER
of the function the macro emitted (10 for 105) and by a binding of a module it emitted (7 for 100); and `comptime(X
+ 1i32)` over the caller's local `K` folded the MODULE's `K` (101). Around it: **DEF-221** -- `#caller(n)` written
in an INNER invocation's argument (`macro:outer = () { #inc(#caller(n)); };`) was consumed into a marked identifier
when the outer body was cloned and LOST the mark when the inner macro cloned its argument: RESOLVE-002, or beside a
module `n` the module's value in silence (101 for 6). The clone carries the marks. **DEF-222** -- `#caller` written
in an argument OUTSIDE every macro body (`#twice(#caller(x))` in ordinary code) was consumed like a body's and the
program compiled; it is `NITPICK-MACRO-008` at the form, as it is everywhere else outside a body. And one finding
withdrawn: **DEF-220, registered by this landing's first reading and WITHDRAWN by its first measurement** --
`macro:w = () { #inner(); };` lets `inner`'s `#caller(k)` read `w`'s CALLER (5) where `{ #inner() + 0i32; }` reads
the module's (100), and the first reading called that two sites out. It is the ALIAS rule: a body that is nothing
but one invocation is whatever its target is (D-125; MACRO_REFERENCE section 1), and the tree's own
`macro_alias_sites.npk` bumps its caller's `acc` through one. The counted roots KEEP it (the alias's target's frame
inherits what `#caller` reaches); the first build did not, and the chain refused that test. Asked of the VERIFIED
build (`.internal/vp/`): **DEF-223, A SOUNDNESS HOLE OF THE VERIFIED BUILD, OLDER THAN THIS LANDING AND NEEDING NO
MACRO** -- the SMT encoder keeps a function's locals by SPELLING, innermost first, and looked every identifier up
that way. (a) A callee's contract at a call site: `requires d > lim` over the MODULE's `lim` (5), called as
`safe(5i32)` from a function with a local `lim` (0), was proven against the caller's local; the call went past the
checked entry and the body -- its division proven safe under the requirement -- divided by zero: the verified binary
exits 107 (`MachineFault`) where the plain build exits 115 (`RequiresViolated`), on `eaf6b08` and every compiler
since the rows existed. (b) Two locals of one spelling that hygiene crosses: `10i32 / #caller(tmp)` (the caller's
`tmp` is 0) in a body that declared its own `tmp` (5) had its `div-zero` row DISCHARGED over the body's (107 for 97)
-- and through an argument the same from the day D-340 lets one be passed, which is why the fix is in this landing.
THE FIX, two rules in `smt_encode.npk`: an identifier the resolver bound to a module-scope declaration never reads a
local's binding (`ident_bind`, at the four sites that turn an identifier node into a binding); and a spelling that
an identifier inside an expansion uses and the function declares more than once is never NAMED (`enc_prepare` puts
it in the escape set DEF-14 built: every read opaque, no version) -- found on the walk DEF-14 needs complete.

**The mechanism.** The expander marks the ROOT of every argument it substitutes with how far out it was written --
one step back per expansion it is substituted into, and for a declaration macro's argument `HYG_SITE` (module-level
text) with one step per module the macro's body emitted around it -- the clone CARRIES a node's marks (a fresh
node's flag word starts empty: the second defect below), `instantiate_expr` composes the marks that meet on one node
into one order (the steps back, `HYG_SITE`, the roots; a root followed by a step back cancels, which is a body that
is exactly a parameter), and the resolver's one `caller_scope` field is a chain of site frames: an expansion's root
pushes the scope it is reached in, an argument's root steps back to the frame below and resolves there, and what
stands under it -- a `pick` arm's binding, an invocation of its own, a `#caller` an enclosing body wrote -- sees the
sites as they were where the argument was written. A frame holds two scopes -- where an argument written at the site
resolves, and what `#caller` reaches -- because an ALIAS (a body that is nothing but one invocation, D-125) roots
two expansions at one node: its target's `#caller` reaches the alias's own caller, as it always did, while what the
alias's body wrote in the target's argument list steps back to the module's scope. A count that does not fit the
sites is `RESOLVE-INTERNAL`, said, never a fallback to the module's scope (which is the wrong answer the marks exist
to end). Files: `src/frontend/ast.npk` (the marks: `HYG_SITE`, `hyg_back`, `hyg_roots`, `hyg_with`, `HYG_CARRY`,
`HYG_PLACED`, the mark accessors; `ast_adopt_expr_flags` retired), `src/frontend/macro/expand.npk`
(`clone_expr`/`clone_stmt` as carrying wrappers, `clone_arg`, the composition in `instantiate_expr`, the block's
marks in `instantiate_stmt`, `at_decl_site` and `mod_depth` in the splices, MACRO-008 for `#caller` inside an
argument), `src/frontend/resolve.npk` (`sites`, `site_push`, `site_enter`, `caller_scope()`, the module frame),
`src/backend/smt/smt_encode.npk` (`ident_bind`, `enc_prepare`'s ambiguity rule), one comment in
`src/frontend/type_resolve.npk`.

**Tests.** `tests/backend/programs/macro_arg_shadow.npk` (eight shapes the parent compiles and answers wrongly, and
the alias control: it exits 1 there) and `macro_arg_site.npk` (twelve functions, a declaration macro in an inline
module and one in an `impl`, and a coroutine twin the parent refuses), both exit 0 at both legs;
`tests/expansion/rejection/argument_site.npk` (RESOLVE-002 x3 and the MACRO-009 note) and `caller_in_argument.npk`
(MACRO-008); `tests/types/rejection/comptime_argument.npk` (TYPE-004 and its note);
`tests/verify/contract_module_name.npk`, `hyg_caller_shadow.npk`, `hyg_arg_shadow.npk` (each row `open` where the
parent discharged it; the verified binary answers 115, 97, 97).

**Measurements.** THE SWEEP under landing 91's checker and this one over 3,635 files (the tree's units and every
.npk of the library and application repositories): 30 sites vanished and 6 appeared, every one read -- in our own
new test files, and in two probe files of nitpick-posix, the listener's own probes of this defect
(`probe02a_failsafe_macro.npk`: RESOLVE-002 at the argument gone, and REACH-001 in its place -- the reach analysis
does not see a `pick` an expansion's block holds, the doubt the probe states; `probe02c_caller_arg.npk`:

TYPE-004 38 → 39; note:MACRO-009 33 → 31 (2,486 -> 2,462 sites); no TRAP or TIMEOUT. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 551
programs under both compilers, 548 byte-identical, 2 different (the two new tests the parent compiles to a wrong
answer: `macro_arg_shadow.npk`, `hyg_arg_shadow.npk`), 0 newly refused, 1 refused by the parent alone

### DEF-225 — LANDED 2026-10-01 (`nitpick-compiler_30`; worktree `wt/30a`, branch `30-def225` on `2b5ef34`; landing 93; the library listener's O-N34)

**Why it was started at the stop.** Landing 92 was the planned last landing of the week's Fable allowance. With 90, 91
and 92 on main and the meter at 87% (his stop line 95%), the user said (2026-10-01, about 16:00): "we aren't at the
95% threshhold yet so if there is anything you can do to use up those last few percent please feel free", and that the
hand-off after it goes to `nitpick-compiler_31`, on Opus, "to log any messages from the libs agents but not act on
them until token reset happens". The library seat had just sent one finding (its O-N34); by the queue's own order
("whatever the finders registered" first) it was the item, and it was sized by measurement before a line was written.

**The defect** (found by nitpick-time's 0.2.2 planning; measured by the listener at `5fbaf4a` and `c15422e`,
reproduced at `2b5ef34` with the ladder's own `build/npkc`): `if (r.is_error) { #unreachable(); }` followed by a read
of `r.value` is `NITPICK-TAINT-001`, where the same arm ending in `exit` compiles and `r ?| #unreachable()` compiles.
The listener read it against D-121 ("an arm that leaves contributes nothing to the merge") and the builtin's row (it
traps and produces no value): a defect, not a rule. Both citations were checked against the tree before the reading
was taken.

**Measured first** (`wt/30a/.internal/probes`, fifteen programs, base = `2b5ef34`'s binaries built in the pristine
worktree): the root is one row missing from two lists. The bindings analysis decides "does this statement leave"
(`stmt_exits`, asked by the `if` merge and the `pick` merge) and "may this statement complete" (`stmt_completes`,
FLOW-001) from lists of statement KINDS, and `#unreachable()` is an EXPRESSION -- as a statement of its own it was "an
expression statement". So the one finding had five faces, each beside a control that compiles (`p03`, the same arm
ending in `!!! Unreachable;`): `p01`/`p08` TAINT-001 (the `if`, the negated test with the leaver in `else`, a nested
block); `p05`/`p06d` ASSIGN-001 (the binding the other arm assigned, through an `if` and through a `pick`); `p12`
ASSIGN-002 (a `fixed` binding written in the arm and again below it); `p07` MOVE-001 (a move in the arm, a read below
it); `p04`/`p10`/`p11` FLOW-001 (a body whose last statement is the builtin: a plain function, a coroutine, `main`),
under a message that says "without `pass`, `fail`, `exit` or a trap".

**The fix.** `stmt_is_unreachable` (bindings.npk): an expression statement whose expression is the builtin. Both
predicates read it beside the trap statement's row -- `stmt_exits` answers that it leaves, `stmt_completes` that it
never completes. ONLY THE BARE STATEMENT: the builtin under another expression (an initialiser, an argument, an
operand) is not looked at, because deciding it means deciding what is evaluated before it; that costs precision and
nothing else (`p09`, and the two refused sites of `unreachable_edge.npk`). Why it is sound in the direction that
matters (`stmt_exits` wrong towards `true` drops an arm that reaches the merge): the emitter lowers the builtin, in
every build, as the trap call and a block terminator (`ir_expr.npk`), so control never steps past the statement; `p02`
and `unreachable_leaves_trap.npk` take the error path and `failsafe` hears `Unreachable` at both legs. What was read
and deliberately NOT changed: the encoder's `stmt_falls_through` (smt_encode.npk) names `#unreachable()` among the
statements that fall through "as far as this walk claims" -- its claim becomes a hypothesis, so it claims less; the
analysis here feeds refusals alone, so no emission and no row's text depends on it.

**What the probes found beside it: DEF-226, registered OPEN.** The `pick (r.is_error)` form's early exit was never
carried out of the `pick`: `pick (r.is_error) { (true) { exit 10i32; }, (false) { } }` followed by the read is
TAINT-001 with EVERY leaver (`p06b` `exit`, `p06c` `!!!`, `p06` the builtin), on the base and on this compiler alike
-- `assign_pick` takes `must` out of the `pick` and has no such step for `checked`. A different root (the merge, not
the list), a permission whose wrong answer admits a tainted read: registered, not built here.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/unreachable_leaves.npk` -- ten functions and a coroutine pair, one per face and
position (the `if`, the `else`, a nested block, a loop body, definite assignment through `if` and `pick`, the `fixed`
binding, the move, a body's end for an `int32` and a `NIL` function, a coroutine's arm and a coroutine's end), each
run on the path where the builtin is not reached and its value checked: exit 0 at both legs; the compiler before this
one refuses the file at twelve sites under five codes. `unreachable_leaves_trap.npk` -- the error taken: 42 through
`failsafe`'s `Unreachable` arm at both legs (a build that reached the read would answer 11 or 0); refused before
(TAINT-001). `tests/analysis/rejection/unreachable_edge.npk` -- the edge of the rule: TAINT-001 and FLOW-001, one site
each, where the builtin stands under an initialiser, beside nine controls (the parent reports eleven sites there).

**The measurements** (base = `2b5ef34`'s binaries, side by side). THE SWEEP under landing 92's checker and this one
over 3,642 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 22 sites vanished and
0 appeared, every one read -- all in this landing's own three test files (the nine controls of `unreachable_edge.npk`,
the twelve sites of `unreachable_leaves.npk`, the one of `unreachable_leaves_trap.npk`: each a refusal the compiler
before this one made of a correct program); no file of the libraries or the applications moves, and none of the tree's
own beyond those three; per code ASSIGN-001 21 → 17; ASSIGN-002 20 → 18; FLOW-001 21 → 16; MOVE-001 52 → 50; TAINT-001
21 → 12 (2,486 -> 2,464 sites); no TRAP or TIMEOUT. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 553 programs under both compilers, 551
byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 2 refused by the parent alone (the two new programs). The libraries and
applications: 2,608 programs, 1,576 byte-identical, 1,032 refused by both, 0 different, 0 newly refused. D-332's count
over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The program suite by hand: 370 programs at both legs, 361 at their
expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The verify suite: 133
files PASS; the problem text of 133 of 133 is byte-identical under both compilers. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify
--record` at this tree -- 7,025 obligations (3,397 discharged, 2,717 open, 0 budget, 906 unencoded, 5 checker),
matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 unmoved; `nitpick.obligations`
6,730 rows before and 6,730 after, 6,535 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, 0 (symbol, kind) discharged
counts fell. The 195 rows that are not shared are RE-KEYED -- a move of type-id names in 38 functions (the new
function's type takes an id), the same rows per function, verdict for verdict: the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets
of the two manifests are equal; the new function has no row. The frontend's 33 and the emitter's 5 unit tests pass;
The in-process checks 0 failures; the generators current.

**Registered with it.** DEF-226 (above). The advance notice was F35. Refusals REMOVED and none added; no computed
answer, no emission, no floor byte moves; no snapshot refresh. THE COMPILER SIDE PAUSES HERE until the Fable allowance
resets (2026-10-07 20:00): `nitpick-compiler_31` logs what the library seats send and acts on none of it.

### DEF-227 — LANDED 2026-10-08 (`nitpick-compiler_31`; worktree `wt/31a`, branch `31-def227` on `93bcb66`; landing 94; the library listener's F-037 in O-N36; the first landing after the Fable pause, with the pause's log registered)

**Where it started.** The compiler side paused at landing 93 (2026-10-01) until the Fable allowance reset
(2026-10-07 20:00); `nitpick-compiler_31`, on Opus, logged what the library seats sent and acted on none of it
(`.internal/handoff_31/FINDINGS_LOG.md`, nine entries: three seat rotations, the fuzzer's M11 sessions 8 and 9 as
O-N35 and O-N36, Gemini's optimization findings as O-N37, two benchmark rounds as O-N38 and O-N39). The user's go
(2026-10-07, 22:50: "we have a fresh batch of tokens. Lets see what we can do."), the pause note's order (wrong
answers first), and the user's two answers given during the pause (the frac print is a wrong answer, 2026-10-05; the
principle: everything a person or a program reads from a value shows it as used, never as stored).

**Measured first** on `93bcb66`, both legs, every silent finding of the log before a line was written: F-037's
`u1`–`u3` exit 10 (the allocator's 0xAA poison read through the trait object) and its two controls 0; F-047's
`s1`/`s2` exit 10 (the write through `fixed uint8[]` lands) with the controls as stated; F-041's `h1` no exit in 60 s
at 1.16 GB resident; the listener's `tleak` 24,004 bytes live against `tleak_x`'s 28; the frac print "-2 5/8" for
`-(1 3/8)`, and — asked by a probe the listener had not written — TWO stored forms of one value: `(-1) + 5/8` is
{−1, 5, 8} and prints "-1 5/8", `1/8 - 1/2` is {0, −3, 8} and prints "-3/8", `==` says equal, the parts differ.

**The defect** (F-037). `x => dyn T` lowers through `emit_fit` (ir_expr.npk), the one path the implicit coercion
into a `dyn` slot takes, and `emit_fit`'s transfer registers the cell as the statement's temporary (D-246); the cast
NODE was also on `temp_producer`'s list, so the same cell was registered a second time under the cast's own name.
`fnem_temp_take` (ir_func.npk) takes ONE entry per name — "the same name never names two live ones", true of every
producer but this one — so the binding took one registration and the statement's end dropped the other: `dyn
Speaks:d = move(l) => dyn Speaks;` then `d.say()` read 0xAA (170 for 7), with and without `move`, over a POD and
over a struct owning a `string`, at both legs; in a function that returns, the scope's exit freed the cell again
(the parent trips the allocator's check on the new test: exit 95, `Unreachable`, both legs). The implicit coercion
and the direct call answered 7 throughout.

**The fix** is the list: a `dyn` cast is not a producer. Its cell is `emit_fit`'s registration, exactly as the
implicit coercion's is, and a cast that `emit_fit` hands back unchanged (the operand already a `dyn` of the target)
produces nothing new, its operand's own registration standing. One line removed, its reason written where it was.

**The test.** `tests/backend/programs/dyn_cast_owner.npk`: nine roads — a declaration with and without `move`, an
owning struct behind the object, an assignment over a live `dyn` (the old cell dropped, the new kept), a lent and a
moved call argument, a return, a field store, a temporary nobody takes, a coroutine across a suspension — and the
two controls, each in a function that RETURNS so the scope's drops run; exit 0 at both legs; the parent exits 95 at
both legs; heap stats 16 allocations, every one freed.

**The measurements** (base = `93bcb66`'s binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 93's checker and this one over 5,060
files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories; 1,418 more than landing 93's
3,642, because the fuzzer's M11 session 9 programs and its findings joined them): 0 sites vanished, 0 appeared,

tests: the frontend's 33 and the emitter's 5 pass. the floor's 388 unmoved; the in-process checks 0 failures.

explicit `=> dyn` cast (the second slot, flag and stores are gone) and for no other; a COMPUTED ANSWER changes where
such a program read the poison. No floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in advance (NOTICES F36).

### DEF-228 and DEF-241 — LANDED 2026-10-08 (built by `nitpick-compiler_31`, documented and landed by `nitpick-compiler_32`; worktree `wt/31b`, branch `31-def228` on `05a7b02`; landing 95; the library listener's O-N38 item 1)

**Where it started.** The listener's benchmark round (entry 7 of the pause's log, `.internal/handoff_31/FINDINGS_LOG.md`):
`string:s = \`n&{i}\`;` run 1,000 times printed `heap: allocated=27890 peak_live=24004 count=2000` under
`NPK_HEAP_STATS`, where the explicit `string_concat("n", int_to_string(i))` prints `peak_live=28`; "no library's code
uses `&{ }`, and the one mention is a comment saying it is avoided on purpose". Registered as DEF-228 by landing 94,
which measured it at `93bcb66` first: 24,004 bytes live against 28.

**The defect.** `emit_template` (ir_expr.npk) rendered each non-string item through `emit_tostring` and joined the
parts with `npk_string_concat`, which copies both operands and frees neither — and dropped nothing: one owned block per
spliced value and one per intermediate concatenation, never freed, invisible to D-151 (managed storage). The template's
FINAL value was the statement's temporary (`temp_producer` lists the literal); its pieces were nobody's. **Beside it,
found probing the lowering (DEF-241):** a template whose ONLY item is a string was handed that item's header through as
its value — `string:t = \`&{ s }\`;` made `t` and `s` one body with two owners, and a function's return freed it twice
(the allocator's check, exit 95, `Unreachable`, both legs); a lone string-typed CALL item was the same body under two
registrations, F-037's shape.

**The fix** is D-246's own mechanism: every `to_string` result and every replaced intermediate concatenation is
registered as the statement's temporary once it has been concatenated past (`temp_register`) and dropped at the
statement's end; a string ITEM is never registered (a place's header is borrowed, a call's result is that call's own
temporary); the final value is registered once, by the producer rule, as before. A lone string PLACE or CALL item is
COPIED (`npk_string_concat` with the empty header), so the template's value is a body of its own. **And one edge the
landing's first form got wrong, found by `nitpick-compiler_32` reading its emission comparison:** a lone LITERAL — a
template with nothing to splice, `\`plain\`` — was copied too, an allocation per evaluation where `"plain"` costs none;
the fuzzer's `op0391` ("a backtick template with nothing interpolated is its text") had moved and said so. A lone
literal is the literal's own header, as a plain literal is (D-049): nothing owns a constant, so nothing is aliased and
nothing is freed. A splice-free template's emission is byte-identical to landing 94's.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/template_owner.npk`: ten roads — the lone string item (DEF-241's shape, 3 + 3
where the parent trapped), the lone spliced value, text-value-text-string (the listener's shape with a tail), two
string items then a value, a string-typed call item, a template inside a template, the value leaving with the function
and going straight into a call, the coroutine twin across a suspension, the empty template, the lone literal — each in a
function that RETURNS so the scope's drops run; exit 0 at both legs; the parent exits 95; 48 allocations, every one
freed. `template_churn.npk` (1,000 trips of `n&{ i }-&{ s }`) against `template_once.npk` under
`tests/cost/template_splice.toml`: peak_live 42 against 36, within the bound of 2 (the parent: 32,789 against 36).
`template_literal_churn.npk` (1,000 splice-free templates) against `template_literal_plain.npk` (1,000 plain literals)
under `tests/cost/template_literal.toml`: 0 against 0, bound 1 — neither allocates. The listener's `tleak`: 24,004 → 28
bytes live.

**The measurements** (base = `05a7b02`'s binaries, landing 94's). THE SWEEP under landing 94's checker and this one over
5065 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 0 sites vanished,
0 appeared, 3117 sites → 3117; no TRAP or TIMEOUT. THE EMISSION COMPARISON:
559 programs under both compilers, 553 byte-identical, 6 different (`char_tostring.npk`, `derive_generic.npk`, `template_churn.npk`, `template.npk`, `template_once.npk`, `template_owner.npk` — every one
holding a template with a splice), 0 newly refused, 0 refused by both. The libraries and
applications: 3976 programs, 2393 byte-identical, 1542 refused by both, 41 different
(8 in nitpick-fuzz; 33 in nitpick-time, every one holding a template with a splice, a derived `ToString`/`Debug` body, or importing a module that does), 0 newly refused. D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched
files. The verify suite's problem text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both
compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 376
programs at both legs, 367 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's
driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's 33 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE
MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree — 7025 obligation(s): 3397 discharged, 2717 open, 0 budget, 906 unencoded, 5 checker, matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself
byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations` 6730 rows before and 6730 after,
6636 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts
fell (the 94 rows not shared are RE-KEYED — a move of type-id names across 23 functions, `float_round`'s 20 among them; the (symbol, kind, tier, verdict) multisets of the two manifests are equal); the floor's 388 unmoved; the in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** No refusal added or removed. The emitted TEXT moves for every program whose emission
holds a template with a splice — written in the program, in a module it imports, or generated by a derive (a derived
`ToString` or `Debug` body is a template) — and for no other: a splice-free template's emission is landing 94's. A

### DEF-229 — LANDED 2026-10-08 (built by `nitpick-compiler_31`, documented and landed by `nitpick-compiler_32`; worktree `wt/31c`, branch `31-def229` on landing 95's commit; landing 96; the library listener's F-041 in O-N36; D-347, D-348 and D-349 recorded; the install README placed)

**Where it started.** F-041 (entry 5 of the pause's log): `deep<T>` calling `deep::<Box<T>>(…)` — "THE COMPILER DOES NOT
TERMINATE": no exit at 60 s at 1.17 GB there, 4.4 GB at 300 s in the fuzzer; the bounded control compiles.
TRAITS_REFERENCE §3.5 promises the cap of 64 "with the instantiation stack printed". Registered as DEF-229 by landing 94,
which measured it at `93bcb66` first: no exit in 60 s at 1.16 GB resident.

**The defect.** The cap existed in ONE place — `resolve_named`'s recursion through nested type ARGUMENTS
(`NITPICK-TYPE-018`, `r.depth` against `GENERIC_DEPTH`) — and the emitter's transitive monomorphization consulted no
depth: `fninst_concrete` (ir_expr.npk) records the concrete instance a specialization's call asks for and
`emit_program`'s loop re-reads the table's count, so `deep<Box<Box<…>>>` was recorded without end, where
type_resolve.npk's comment had promised that "Phase B's monomorphizer inherits the same constant".

**The fix: two refusals, one code.** (1) THE CHECKER, at the call (`type_generic_call`, type_members.npk): a function's
direct self-call whose type argument is not the bare parameter and mentions one of the function's OWN parameters
(`type_mentions_params_of`, with the declaration's window — an enclosing impl's `U` is somebody else's: `f<T>` calling
`f::<Box<U>>` inside `impl:<U>` is one instance per `U`, finite) is `NITPICK-TYPE-018` naming the function and the type,
since every instance asks for one nested a level deeper and no expansion of it ends. (2) THE EMITTER'S CAP, for the
indirect shape (`f<T>` → `g::<Box<T>>` → `f::<U>`): `fninst_concrete` and `note_family_instance` measure the requested
arguments' nesting (`type_nest_depth`: a leaf is 1, a kind with operands one more than the deepest of them, failing
CLOSED when its fuel is spent) against `GENERIC_DEPTH` (`pub` now: one number for both halves) and record nothing past
it; the sentence waits in the emitter (`ExprEmitter.inst_refusal`) and the asking call — or the specialization being
emitted, for a family method — carries it out as `iv_refused`, a new `IrVal` state (`LL_REFUSED`: an emitter refusal by
NAME, beside the rung and the defect states), which `emit_program`'s `refuse` pushes as the code and the sentence it
carries. The indirect probe is refused in 0.34 s where the parent never exited. `type_mentions_param` moved from
ir_types.npk into types.npk beside the two new walks (`type_mentions_params_of`, `type_kind_has_elem`). The walkers-total instrument (`check_type_walkers_total`, harness.py) follows the move: its one entry for the walk
became three — `type_mentions_params_of`, `type_kind_has_elem` and `type_nest_depth` — so every type kind is named
by exactly one of the pair or excused with its reason, and a kind dropped from the predicate fails the predicate's own
entry (the instrument's first run on this landing's tree said `type_mentions_param` not found in ir_types.npk: the
entry was stale, not the compiler).

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/generic_self_growth.npk`: four growing self-calls — `Box<T>`, `Pair<T, int32>`,
`T->`, a second parameter growing while the first stays — beside four controls (the bare parameter, a concrete type, an
enclosing impl's parameter, a growing argument handed to a function that does not call back); the parent reports nothing
on the file. `tests/backend/programs/generic_nesting_bounded.npk`: the shapes the cap leaves alone — the fuzzer's
three-deep control, a cycle that closes at a concrete type, a self-call at the bare parameter; exit 0 at both legs. The
emitter's refusal has no `[[test]]` kind that expects a backend refusal; it is measured by hand: the fuzzer's `h1` is
TYPE-018 in a third of a second, its `ctl_h2` unchanged.

**Recorded with it.** D-347 (the frac representation: stored = read = printed) and D-348 (a `fixed` slice is read-only
through it, in two steps) — the user's "your recommendations are fine with me" of 2026-10-08 00:33, in
`nitpick-compiler_31`'s session — and D-349 (the toolchain pin moves to the LLVM 20.1 release the distributions serve,
20.1.8, with NIKOS and Alive2 rebuilt and re-measured against it: his "we just can't forget the dependency chain beyond
just the compiler itself" of 01:15; S-131 settled). THE INSTALL README: `INSTALL.md` at the root with `README.md`'s
pointer (the third thing he ratified at 00:33), drafted by `nitpick-compiler_31` from a fresh-clone run and corrected by

**The measurements** (base = landing 95's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 95's checker and this one over 5069 files
(the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 0 sites vanished, 6
appeared (the finding's own `h1_unbounded_instantiation.npk` and the fuzzer's claim program `tr0586.npk` (TRAITS §3.5's claim, 'expect: refuse') -- each a direct self-growing call the parent's CHECKER passed clean, the hang being the emitter's -- and `generic_self_growth.npk`'s four sites), 3117 sites → 3123; per code NITPICK-TYPE-018 0 → 6. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 560
programs under both compilers, 560 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly
refused, 0 refused by both. The libraries and applications: 3978 programs (two more than at landing 95: the library seat's work in progress; one listed file, `nitpick-regex/tests/unit/parse_fuzz.npk`, vanished between the listing and the run and reads as refused by both), 2435

files PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 377 programs at both legs, 368 at their expected
exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's

### DEF-231 — LANDED 2026-10-08 under D-347 (built by `nitpick-compiler_31`, documented and landed by `nitpick-compiler_32`; worktree `wt/31d`, branch `31-def231` on landing 96's commit; landing 97; the fuzzer's `ty1657` in O-N36)

**Where it started.** The fuzzer's `ty1657` (entry 5 of the pause's log, M11's TYPE run): `-(1 3/8)` printed "-2 5/8"
and `-(2 5/8)` printed "-3 3/8", "which any reader of mixed numbers takes for −2.625". The user ruled the print a wrong
answer on 2026-10-05 ("if you are printing a thing out you want the readable representation most of the time, not
details about how it's stored"), gave the principle the same day ("we don't like surprises, especially when safety
could be involved. we also don't want to force people into doing conversions in their head when working with the
fractions"), preferred the stored form BECOMING the readable one ("the less conversions there are the better for
everyone"), and ratified the recommendation on 2026-10-08 ("your recommendations are fine with me"): D-347. Landing 94
registered it as DEF-231 and, measuring at `93bcb66`, found the second face: ONE VALUE HAD TWO STORED FORMS — `(-1) +
5/8` was {−1, 5, 8} (printing "-1 5/8") and `1/8 - 1/2` was {0, −3, 8}; `==` said equal, the parts differed.

**The defect.** D-198's five invariants carried the prototype's floor form — "num ≥ 0 when whole ≠ 0; the sign rides
whole (or num when whole = 0)" — with no reason of its own recorded anywhere; `npk_frac_norm`'s last step BORROWED from
the whole so the fraction was never negative beside a nonzero whole (−1 3/8 became {−2, 5, 8}), left a value with a
zero whole alone ({0, −3, 8}) and a value that arrived as {−1, 5, 8} as it was — two forms of one number. Everything
that READ the parts (the members, a `Debug` through `to_string`, a derived `Hash`) saw two numbers for one, and the
print showed the stored parts as they were.

**The fix (D-347).** The stored parts ARE the readable parts: `whole` and `num` never of opposite signs (one may be
zero), |num| < denom, gcd(|num|, denom) = 1 with denom = 1 when num = 0, and value = whole + num/denom in every case —
one form per value by construction. `npk_frac_norm`'s last block (prelude.npk): a positive whole beside a negative
fraction borrows one (1 − 3/8 is {0, 5, 8}); a negative whole beside a positive fraction carries one ((−1) + 5/8 is
{0, −3, 8}); a zero whole keeps the fraction's sign, a zero fraction the whole's. The four `ToString` impls print the
sign ONCE — the whole carries it and the fraction beside a nonzero whole is printed as its magnitude: "-1 3/8", "-3/8",
"0", "ERR"; `Debug` prints the same. `emit_frac_cast`'s integer exit (ir_expr.npk) is the whole field alone — its "plus
one when whole is negative and num nonzero" was the floor form's correction. `npk_frac_cmp` needed nothing: it compares
improper forms, one number under either form. The prelude is embedded through the generated `prelude_source.npk` and
was regenerated (the landing's first build carried the old prelude in silence until it was). `frac_basic.npk`'s floor-form
expectations follow the form; TYPE_REFERENCE's invariant sentence, members' row and `ToString` row say the rule, with
D-198's dated note.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/frac_parts.npk`, thirty-three checks: −(1 3/8) is {−1, −3, 8}, prints "-1 3/8",
casts to −1 and −1.375; −(2 5/8) is {−2, −5, 8}, prints "-2 5/8" (which IS −2.625), casts to −2 and −2.625; −3/8 by
three routes — `(-1) + 5/8`, `1/8 - 1/2`, `-(3/8)` — is one form, prints "-3/8", the routes compare equal, casts to 0;
the positive twins unchanged; a borrow (1 − 3/8 = 5/8) and a carry ((−2) + 11/8 = −5/8); −1, 0 and ERR keep their
forms; the order unchanged (−2 5/8 < −1 3/8 < −3/8 < 0 < 5/8 < 1 3/8); `frac64` renders alike; exit 0 at both legs.
Every frac program of the tree at both legs: `frac_basic`, `frac_cmp_err` (its expected 24), `complex_basic`,
`scalar_traits`, `module_constants`, `pick_fixed_point`, `tfp_tostring` (its 42). The fuzzer's `ty1657` exits 0 (11 on
the parent, which prints the old form).

**The measurements** (base = landing 96's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 96's checker and this one over 5,069 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 5 sites vanished and 5 appeared -- one thing, read: the three `note:` lines of `list_fields.npk` and the two of the fuzzer's `me0068`/`me0068b` that point INTO the prelude, at the `List` fields' declarations, by line (`prelude.npk:2221`-`2223` -> `2248`-`2250`: the frac section grew by 27 lines above them); no site of any program moved; 3,123 sites both ways; no TRAP or TIMEOUT. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 561 programs under both compilers, 0 byte-identical, 561 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by both -- EVERY emission moves, and the cause was read before a word of this was written: D-179's error-origin SITE TABLE. Every program's emission carries the prelude's WHOLE table (287 `prelude.npk` sites at landing 96, in a program that calls none of them), numbered before the program's own sites; the prelude's frac section gained six guard sites (the eight `overflow` guards of the new code -- the four arithmetic steps of `npk_frac_norm`'s borrow and carry, the `0i64 - n` of the four `to_string` bodies -- for the two it replaced) and grew by 27 lines, so in every program the `@npk.sitep.N` rows, the `@npk.site.paths` and `@npk.site.lines` tables, the site argument of every `npk_chain_reset`/`npk_chain_push` and the lookup's bound move: every program site's id by six, every prelude site's line below the edit by 27.
