### DEF-230 — LANDED 2026-10-08 under D-348 step (i) (`nitpick-compiler_32`; worktree `wt/32a`, branch `32-def230` on landing 97's commit; landing 98; the library listener's F-047 in O-N36)

**Where it started.** F-047 (entry 5 of the pause's log): "A SILENT WRONG ANSWER. A `fixed uint8[]` accepts a write, and
the write lands: a callee writing through its `fixed uint8[]` parameter changes the caller's bytes. D-074 says 'an
immutable byte view is `fixed uint8[]`'." Its `s1`/`s2` exit 10 at both compilers on both legs; its controls: a plain view
writes, as it may, and a `fixed` scalar is ASSIGN-002; "no library uses a fixed slice view". Landing 94 registered it as
DEF-230 with S-129 (measured at `93bcb66`: exit 10 at both legs) and the user settled S-129 as D-348 on 2026-10-08:
D-074's promise is made true, in two steps.

**The defect.** `place_fixed` (type_stmt.npk) — the one question every write form asks through `refuse_fixed_write`
(TYPE-086) and every address form asks directly (`@`, `$$i`/`$$m`, the pointer-receiver call: TYPE-071) — answered false
for an index whose base is a slice, by D-287's reading ("the storage there is not the binding's own"), as it does for a
pointer and a handle. So `fixed` fixed the view's HEADER and not the bytes, and `fixed uint8[]:v` written through
(`v[0i64] = 9u8`) changed the caller's bytes in silence, while D-074 had retired `binary` on the promise that an
immutable byte view is `fixed uint8[]` and TYPE_REFERENCE §22 taught it. The checker's reading and the reference's
promise disagreed; the user chose the promise.

**The fix (step (i)).** The walk goes THROUGH a slice base to the view's root — a pointer and a handle still stop it,
because an address is not a view — so an element of a slice rooted at a `fixed` binding, held in a `fixed` aggregate or
declared a `fixed` field is fixed storage to every form: an element write, a compound assignment and a write through a
sub-range are `NITPICK-TYPE-086`; `@`, `$$i`/`$$m` and a pointer-receiver call on an element are `NITPICK-TYPE-071`.
`place_through_slice` tells the view's sentence from the binding's — "a `fixed` slice is read-only through it (D-074,
D-348): the bytes it views are written by no path that spells `fixed`" — at `refuse_fixed_write` and the three address
sites, because D-287's sentence (read-only memory, a `constant` global) is not the reason here. The two codes' comments
and `place_fixed`'s say the rule; `place_sealed_access` keeps stopping at a slice, and its comment says why a `sealed`
field's question and a `fixed` view's differ.

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/fixed_slice_write.npk`: nine sites — a local `fixed uint8[]` view written (the
listener's `s1`), a callee writing through its `fixed uint8[]` parameter (`s2`), a compound assignment, a write through a
sub-range, a slice held in a `fixed` struct, a `fixed` slice FIELD of a plain struct, `@` of an element, `$$m` of an
element, a pointer-receiver call on an element — beside the silent controls: a plain view written, a `fixed` view read,
ranged and passed to a reader, and a `fixed` view handed to a writer taking a PLAIN view (the hole step (ii) closes; it
compiles today). The header was measured to a fixed point and every site read against its line. The listener's `s1`/`s2`
are TYPE-086, `ctl_s3` exits 0 and `ctl_s4` is ASSIGN-002 as before; its `ty1719` (a `fixed` view binds and reads)
compiles and `ty1719b` (the write) is TYPE-086. Every `fixed uint8[]` of the tree — the `Writer` trait's `write`
parameter in the prelude's four impls, the compiler's `diag_writer.npk` and `impl_foreign_b.npk` — compiles as before.

**The measurements** (base = landing 97's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 97's checker and this one over 5,072 files
(the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 1 site vanished (NITPICK-REACH-001 at the new rejection file's own `failsafe`, a site that exists only because the PARENT admits the file's nine writes and runs its reach analysis over it, which asks the `failsafe` to name what can reach it; this compiler refuses the writes first), 12
appeared (the nine sites of `fixed_slice_write.npk` -- TYPE-086 at its six write forms, TYPE-071 at its three address forms -- and the finding's own reproducers, TYPE-086 each: the listener's `s1_fixed_view_written.npk` and `s2_fixed_param_written.npk` and the fuzzer's `ty1719b.npk`; no other file of the tree, the libraries or the applications moves), 3,124 sites → 3,135; per code NITPICK-REACH-001              8 ->     7; NITPICK-TYPE-071              55 ->    58; NITPICK-TYPE-086              38 ->    47. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 561
programs under both compilers, 561 byte-identical, 0 different (none), 0 newly
refused, 0 refused by both. The libraries and applications: 3,977 programs, 2,432
byte-identical, 1,542 refused by both, 0 different (none), 3 newly refused
(`s1_fixed_view_written.npk`, `s2_fixed_param_written.npk`, `ty1719b.npk`). D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The verify suite's problem
text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133
files PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 378 programs at both legs, 369 at their expected
exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's
unit tests: the frontend's 33 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree — 7,050 obligations (3,417 discharged, 2,722 open, 0 budget, 906 unencoded, 5 checker),
matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations`
6,752 rows before and 6,754 after, 6,645 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows,
ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell; the two rows that are new are `place_through_slice`'s (one `overflow` discharged -- `fuel - 1i32` under its `fuel <= 0` guard -- and the `stack-depth` row of its recursive group, `open` as D-304 (5) accepts for the tree's 126 others), and the 107 rows that are not shared are RE-KEYED -- a move of type-id names in 26 functions of `smt_encode`, `smt_writer`, `ir_expr`, `ir_types`, `alias` and `numeric`, the same rows per function, verdict for verdict: the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests differ by exactly those two new rows; the floor's 388 unmoved; the in-process checks
0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A REFUSAL ADDED: `NITPICK-TYPE-086` at a write through a `fixed` slice and
`NITPICK-TYPE-071` at an address of its element, each with the view's own sentence; no file of the tree, the libraries
or the applications is refused anew but the finding's own reproducers. No emission moves for a program that compiles on
both compilers. No floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in advance (NOTICES F40). What step (i) leaves — a `fixed
uint8[]` handed to a PLAIN parameter whose callee writes — is D-348's step (ii), `fixed T[]` as a TYPE that a plain view
converts to and never back: a language change before the freeze, PLANNED before it is built (the README's Remaining
list).

**The landing's own path, for the record.** Built, tested and documented by `nitpick-compiler_32` in `wt/32a` on the
stack above landing 97; its chain and re-record ran on that tree, the numbers were written in, and the harness ran on
the final tree.

### D-349 — LANDED 2026-10-08 (`nitpick-compiler_32`; worktree `wt/32b`, branch `32-d349` on landing 98's commit; landing 99; S-131, the library seat's VM test of the install README)

**Where it started.** The library seat ran INSTALL.md on a fresh Ubuntu 26.04 machine (2026-10-08) and found that no package
source serves LLVM 20.1.2 any more -- apt.llvm.org's noble suite and Ubuntu 26.04's archive both ship 20.1.8 -- so `npkg`
refused the only toolchain a newcomer can install ("llc is 20.1.8 but nitpick.toml pins 20.1.2"). S-131 put the choice to
the user; D-349 is his answer of 01:15: the pin moves to the release the distributions serve, "we just can't forget the
dependency chain beyond just the compiler itself".

**Measured first, with the machine untouched.** apt.llvm.org's signed noble-20 packages (`1:20.1.8~++20250804090239+87f0227cb601-1~exp1~20250804210352.139`;
`InRelease` verified with `gpgv`, the `Packages` index held to it, every `.deb` to the index) were extracted into a user prefix
(`dpkg -x`; the tools' RUNPATH is `$ORIGIN/../lib`, and the `libLLVM.so.20.1` link that makes it work ships in `llvm-20-dev`, so
that package was in -- `ldd` on the prefix's `llc` resolves the prefix's library, and `--version` is printed by the library that
loaded) and the whole ladder run under `PATH=<prefix>/usr/lib/llvm-20/bin` on landing 98's tree, beside the 20.1.2 ladder:

| row | 20.1.8 | 20.1.2 (the record step's ladder at 98) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| the datalayout `opt -S` derives from the pinned triple | the pinned string | the pinned string | identical |
| `npkrt.o` (the floor, `llc -O0`) | `c8e5033a…` 72,656 B | `c8e5033a…` 72,656 B | IDENTICAL |
| `npkx.o` (the explorer shim) | `65bac639…` | `65bac639…` | IDENTICAL |
| `builder.o` (`stage1.ll` through `llc -O0`) | `5870b02d…` 12,067,840 B | `5870b02d…` | IDENTICAL |
| `builder` (`ld.lld -static`) | `b4dff7b3…` 10,402,536 B | `98f91fec…` 10,402,536 B | ONE BYTE: `.comment` "Linker: Ubuntu LLD 20.1.8" for "20.1.2" |
| `npkc.ll` -- THE EMISSION, by the 20.1.8-built builder | `dcca97cb…` 31,443,507 B | `dcca97cb…` | BYTE-IDENTICAL |
| `npkc.o` | `1d3348d8…` 12,945,224 B | `1d3348d8…` | IDENTICAL |
| `npkc` | `a5a9415e…` 11,189,360 B | `672c8381…` 11,189,360 B | the same ONE `.comment` byte |
| `dyn_slots` through the 20.1.8-built `npkc`, `llc -O0` and `opt -O2`+`llc -O2` | exit 42, exit 42 | 42 expected | as expected |
| `float_vectors.py --llvm` (LLVM's decimal parser, outside every gate) | 8,890 within the cap: 0 differ; 8 past it: 4 differ | the same | identical |
| the self-hosting fixpoint (the `selfhost` pair: T2 == T3) | holds, 31,444,215 B (`2cf259ab…`); the stage objects identical across the two `llc`s | holds | identical |

So a patch release of LLVM 20 moved EXACTLY the linker's version string in `.comment` of every linked binary -- no object, no
emission, no derived layout, no floor byte, no shim byte. D-265's claim (the emission holds across toolchain BUILDS) held of the
objects too, this once.

**The dependency chain (the user's condition).** NIKOS (`db47f9d`, the user's port: LLVM static) and Alive2 (`02ec3af8` with the recorded `rlimit` patch; links `libLLVM.so.20.1` dynamically) rebuilt against 20.1.8 and re-pinned: `ikos-analyzer` 93b4cb15… (13,685,600 B), `ikos-pp` 4b1ad1a9…, `ikos-import` 9c15d2c5… -- NIKOS's build byte-identical between the fresh measuring root and the pinned root (no path enters it); `alive-tv` cdc3870a… (2,021,352 B), `alive-exec` ba8ece21…, `tv.so` 09fd44ff… (the pinned root's: Alive2's rpath carries the root, so the measuring build differed from it by that string alone); z3's `libz3.so` 8a8e6870…, Clam's four rows and the patch's digest unchanged; `engines.sh check` ok on every row. NIKOS's cmake refused the first rebuild -- "LLVM and Clang versions
do not match": its `FindClang` looks for `clang` in the LLVM tools directory, then on PATH, and demands the same release, so the
prefix had to hold apt.llvm.org's `clang-20` (with `libclang-cpp20`, `libclang1-20`, `libclang-common-20-dev`, each verified)
beside `llvm-20-dev` -- a fact for every machine that builds the engines: `llvm.sh 20` installs clang-20, a bare `apt install
llvm-20-dev` does not. Re-measured through the rebuilt engines: the gate's eight planted controls through the rebuilt NIKOS, both modes: 7 of 8 FOUND (`divz`, `null`, `null_known`, `oob_read`, `oob_write`, `uninit`, `wrap_branch`; precisely or imprecisely exactly as 1.6.0 step 3 recorded, line for line), `ctl_uaf` MISSED as then (the free is an external callee it does not know); and over the two programs' plain and whole forms in inter mode NIKOS's verdicts are 1.6.0's to the count -- `dyn_slots.plain` 14 functions / 12 with checks, {ok 1,124, unreachable 200, warning 53}, 53 alarms; `dyn_slots.whole` 201 / 82, {error 3,888, ok 183,754, unreachable 34,426, warning 11,472}, 15,360; `extern_c_driver.plain` 82 / 1, {ok 90, warning 2}, 2; `extern_c_driver.whole` 269 / 45, {error 338, ok 22,822, unreachable 5,915, warning 1,641}, 1,979 -- the intra rows running to the hour cap as they did then; Alive2's smoke (`alive-tv --smt-rlimit=20000000 --smt-random-seed=0`, two runs each, both agreeing) answers 1.6.0's counts exactly: `dyn_slots` {correct 7, incorrect 2, failed-to-prove 3, errors 0} and its no-inlining twin {8, 2, 3, 0}, `extern_c_driver` {52, 13, 15, 1} and {52, 14, 15, 1} -- the two `incorrect` on `dyn_slots` the inlined vtable thunks of 1.6.0's record, Alive2's stated inter-procedural blind spot, not a miscompile; measured with the fresh-root build against the extracted prefix AND with the pinned binary in `~/.local/src/1.6`. `engines.sh` finds its LLVM 20 by
default where PATH's `llc` lives (resolved through its links), the rule both runners use, so the engines are built against the
LLVM the pin check accepts and never against `/usr/lib/llvm-20` by name; `LLVM20=` still overrides it, which is how the
pre-measurement built into a fresh `ENGINES_ROOT` without touching the pinned install.

**The machine's move, and what it found.** The planned route -- `llvm.sh 20` and `apt install lld-20 llvm-20-dev clang-20` with
the user's sudo -- FAILED on his machine: the 32-bit Mesa drivers (`mesa-vulkan-drivers:i386`, `mesa-libgallium:i386`: Steam,
Wine) hold `libllvm20:i386` at the archive's 20.1.2, apt.llvm.org's suite is `amd64 arm64 s390x` with no i386, and a
`Multi-Arch: same` library must be one version across its architectures, so apt kept 20.1.2 and every 20.1.8 package was unmet
("libllvm20 … but 1:20.1.2-0ubuntu1~24.04.3 is to be installed"). Removing the 32-bit drivers was not an option. The release
went in as a PREFIX instead, with no sudo: the ten verified packages extracted into `~/.local/llvm-20.1.8` (byte-identical to
the measuring prefix: `diff -rq` 0 differences), the `~/.local/bin` links repointed for the 39 tools it holds (`FileCheck`,
`not`, `split-file`, `lldb` stay at the system's 20.1.2; `links_before.txt` records the old targets), `/usr/lib/llvm-20` left at
the distribution's 20.1.2 for Mesa and everything else. From 09:40 `llc --version` is 20.1.8 on PATH, every worktree pinned to
20.1.2 refuses until rebased onto this landing, and the library seat's runners -- on their own private 20.1.2 prefix since
the morning (their option (c)) -- never noticed. INSTALL.md documents the i386 trap and the prefix route beside the apt ones.

**The harness.** The landing's full harness ran in `wt/32b` on its WIP commit under the prefix (`PATH=<prefix>/usr/lib/llvm-20/bin`,
the pin check reading "LLVM version 20.1.8" / "LLD 20.1.8" and `nitpick.toml`'s `20.1.8`): green, 53 of 53 --
`programs    401 real-backend program(s)`; `verify      7050 obligation(s): 3417 discharged, 2722 open, 0 budget, 906 unencoded, 5 checker; nitpick.obligations matches; 3417 guard(s) elided; the verified compiler rebuilds itself byte-identically`; `floor       388 floor obligation(s) over 90 specified symbol(s): 381 discharged, 7 budget (residue); runtime/npkrt.obligations matches`; `selfhost    stage 1 rebuilt itself byte-identically`; `repro       emission cwd-independent, llc deterministic`; `datalayout  every module states the pinned layout and triple (nitpick.toml [toolchain]); opt derives the pinned layout from the triple`; `parity      2028 verdict(s) agree between the two runners; npkc byte-identical; the verified compiler byte-identical`. The prefix it ran
under and the one the machine resolves are the same bytes, so this is the landing's harness on the machine's toolchain; the
final commit differs from the harnessed one by documents, `pins.txt` and `engines.sh`, none of which a harness reads, and the
in-process checks were rerun on the final tree. `nitpick.obligations` did not move (no `src/` change; the rows are TEXT), and
neither did the floor's.

**What changes for a program.** Nothing in the language, the checker, the emitter or the floor: NO refusal added or removed, NO
emission moves, no computed answer changes, no floor byte, no snapshot refresh (`STAMP` carries a dated line; the snapshot's
text, digest and byte count stand). The PIN is `20.1.8`: both runners refuse 20.1.2 from this landing and accept 20.1.8; a
machine on Ubuntu 24.04's own archive (20.1.2) takes apt.llvm.org's route or the prefix route; Ubuntu 26.04's archive serves the
pin directly. The library side's CI pins by the upstream tarball's LLVM_VERSION line and re-pins at its own adoption after
DEF-165 and DEF-164; its baseline for this landing is 98's six ladder rows, expecting `npkc.ll` unmoved and the two binaries
moved by the `.comment` byte alone. Registered with it: DEF-243 and DEF-244 (the library seat's O-N40, measured at 98's compiler).

**The landing's own path, for the record.** Measured by `nitpick-compiler_32` on landing 98's tree while 97's and 98's harnesses
ran; the engines rebuilt first into a fresh root against the extracted prefix (step A, with the clang finding), then into the
pinned root against the machine's prefix (step D); the WIP commit's harness under the prefix from 08:47; the user's apt attempt at
~09:30 and the prefix move at 09:40; the documents written in after the measurements; announced in advance (NOTICES F41).

### DEF-165 — LANDED 2026-10-08 under D-337 (`nitpick-compiler_32`; worktree `wt/32c`, branch `32-def165` on landing 99's commit; landing 100; found by D-332's count rule over the library listener's corpus, 2026-09-27)

**Where it started.** D-332's count rule, run over the library listener's corpus on 2026-09-27, showed a struct literal of a
type with several `sealed` fields, written outside the type's module — `nitpick-regex`'s `PatternError{ kind: …, offset: …,
span_len: …, detail: … }` — reported as FOUR `NITPICK-TYPE-079` at one span: D-313 counts a struct literal among the write
forms, and the checker counted it per field. Two readings were put to the user (each sealed field its own fact, or the literal
one mistake, D-240's "one mistake, one report"), and he settled it as D-337 on 2026-09-30: the literal is the mistake —
constructing the type outside the module that owns its invariants — and the fix is one action whatever the count, the owning
module's constructor; ONE report, naming every sealed field the literal writes; its own landing with an advance notice.

**Measured first**, on landing 99's checker: a literal writing four sealed fields from outside their module is four TYPE-079 at
one span with a "declared `sealed` here" note under each field; one writing one sealed field is one; the owning module's
constructor passes (`scratchpad/def165/def165_probe.npk`).

**The defect.** `type_struct_literal` (type_expr.npk) typed the literal's window field by field and asked `report_sealed` for
each `sealed` field it met outside the module — D-313's write form counted per field, where every other write form is one
statement and so one report, and where D-337 reads the literal as one mistake.

**The fix.** The sealed fields the literal writes from outside their module are COLLECTED as the window is typed (a field given
twice — TYPE-028's — counted once) and reported after it: a literal writing ONE keeps `report_sealed`'s sentence (so
`sealed_fields.npk`'s literal and its header stand); one writing SEVERAL is one `NITPICK-TYPE-079` at the literal's span —
"`T`'s `sealed` fields `a`, `b` are written by this struct literal outside the module that declares `T` (D-313, D-337): a
`sealed` field is read anywhere and written only by its module, and a literal that writes several is ONE mistake -- call a
function of that module, which keeps what the seal protects" — with the note "declared `sealed` here" under each field
(`note_sealed_decl`, split out of `report_sealed` in type_stmt.npk and read by both). Every other write form stays one report
per write, each its own statement (D-337); a `hidden` field named in a literal stays one `NITPICK-TYPE-080` per field (D-314:
each touch is its own fact; D-337 speaks of sealed writes alone).

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/sealed_literal.npk`: five literals from outside the owning module — four sealed fields
(the listener's `PatternError` shape), two sealed among plain ones, the values copied from a made value (reads, which pass; the
literal still writes), a sealed field given twice (`NITPICK-TYPE-028` and the one TYPE-079, naming each field once), one sealed
field (the per-field sentence, unchanged) — beside the owning module's own literals and constructors, which pass; the header

### DEF-164 — LANDED 2026-10-08 (`nitpick-compiler_32`; worktree `wt/32d`, branch `32-def164` on landing 100's commit; landing 101; found by D-332's count rule over the library listener's corpus, 2026-09-27, with four more faces found at 1.6.1e step 3 and 3a)

**Where it started.** D-332's count rule, run over the library listener's corpus on 2026-09-27, read `pub error:ETimeValue(ValueFault);`
(`nitpick-time`'s `probe14_error_payload_refused.npk`: an error identity with a payload, which the language does not have) as TWO
`NITPICK-PARSE-001` — "expected `;`, found `(`" and then "expected `:`, found `)`": after the first report the parser's recovery
stopped at the identifier the broken declaration left behind and read `ValueFault)` as a new declaration. D-240: one mistake, one
report. Four more faces of the same family were measured at 1.6.1e step 3 and 3a and registered with it: an integer run with a bad
digit (`12abc`) is `LEX-003` and then the parser's `PARSE-002` "expected an expression" at the same token; a literal outside the
64-bit envelope is `LEX-004` and then `PARSE-002`; a float's dangling exponent sign (`1.5e+;`) is `LEX-009` for `1.5e` and then
`PARSE-002` at the `+` left behind; and an `impl` method whose parameter type is refused (`TYPE-001`) is also `TYPE-014` ("does not
have the signature its trait declares"), the comparison reading the refused type as a mismatch.

**Measured first**, on landing 100's checker: each of the five probes (`scratchpad/def164/`) gives two reports as described.

**The fixes, one per face.** (a) A DECLARATION'S RECOVERY SKIPS TO A SYNC POINT: `p_recover_decl` (parser.npk) skips to `;`, `}` or a
declaration keyword — `is_sync_point`'s set — and steps over the `;`, never resuming at an identifier the broken declaration left
behind; the four declaration loops of parse_decl.npk (the module, an item block, a struct or enum body, an `extern` body) call it, and
statements keep `p_recover`, whose stop at anything that can start an item is right for a statement list. A declaration the recovery
skips is lost to the parse, which costs nothing: a parse diagnostic stops the driver before resolution. (b), (d) A REFUSED INTEGER
LITERAL STAYS AN `IntLit` (lexer.npk), value 0 and its width, as a refused float stays a `FloatLit` since DEF-166 — the parser has an
operand and adds nothing. (c) A DANGLING EXPONENT SIGN BEFORE A TERMINATOR IS THE TAIL'S (`num_float_scan`, numeric.npk): a tail that
is exactly `e`/`E` followed by `+`/`-` and then `;`, `)`, `]`, `}`, `,` or the end of the text takes the sign, so `1.5e+;` is the
literal `1.5e+` refused once (LEX-009 naming `e+`), while `3.5e-x` keeps its reading — the literal `3.5e`, a minus and a name, one
report as before. (e) A SIGNATURE HOLDING A REFUSED TYPE IS NOT COMPARED (`sig_holds_invalid`, type_trait.npk): the TYPE-014
mismatch report is skipped when either function type carries an invalid parameter or return type, since TYPE-001 was reported
where the type stands; a real mismatch beside sound types is TYPE-014 as before.

**The tests.** `tests/frontend/parse_one_report.npk` — a parse refusal is a UNIT case (D-085: the grammar stage feeds every tree file
through the real parser): faces (a) to (d) each lexed and parsed, exactly one diagnostic with its code, beside the controls — the
recovery resumes at the next declaration keyword (the function after the broken declaration is in the module), two broken
declarations are two reports, `3.5e-x` is one report, a sound module parses clean; the file releases its wild storage before `exit`
as its siblings do. `tests/types/rejection/impl_refused_param.npk` — face (e): a refused parameter type and a refused return type,
TYPE-001 once each, beside a real mismatch that is still TYPE-014; the header measured on this checker after the file had its final
line count (TYPE-001 at 22, 23; TYPE-014 at 24), the parent reporting TYPE-014 at 22 and 23 as well.

**The measurements** (base = landing 100's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 100's checker and this one over 5,089 files (the

### 2.10e D-348 step (ii) as it landed — `fixed T[]` a TYPE (landing 103; planned 2026-10-08 by `nitpick-compiler_33` at `.internal/handoff_33/D348_II_PLAN.md`, measured first; S-132 and S-133 ratified the same day as D-350 and D-351)

**What the plan measured before a line was written.** The hole step (i) left — a `fixed uint8[]` handed to a plain
parameter whose callee writes — was one of SIX faces (DEF-246), each a plain view minted from or copied out of `fixed`
storage, measured on landing 100's compiler: `string_bytes` of a `fixed` module string written through (`MachineFault`), a
`fixed` module array ranged and written (`MachineFault`), a `fixed` local array ranged (silent), a view copied out of a
`fixed` struct (silent), a `fixed` view assigned to a plain binding (silent), an impl of `Writer` dropping the trait's
`fixed` (accepted; the caller's bytes written through the bound). Beside them: `fixed` and `stack` as return qualifiers
parsed and were read by nothing (DEF-247); a `fixed` parameter was reassignable (DEF-248, landing 102). The census: 27
slice spellings in `src/`, the libraries' real slice code in nitpick-regex (parse.npk, dump.npk, cursor.npk) and
nitpick-time (vec.npk, bytes.npk); `fixed T[]` spelled in seven tree files and eight fuzz programs; one library function
returning a slice; `string_bytes` called 18 times in `src/`, 157 in nitpick-regex, 30 in nitpick-time, 1,070 in the fuzz
corpus; no view local of the tree or of any library's sources re-assigned; 44 sites switching on `TY_SLICE`, 8 calling
`tt_slice`, 21 calling `fits`.

**The decisions.** (1) The type is `TY_SLICE` with its `b` slot set, never a new kind: every kind-keyed walker keeps its
answer, the id is distinct. (2) The spelling, S-132 → D-350 (R1): `fixed` stays the binding's qualifier at a declaration
and a `fixed` binding of slice type has the type `fixed T[]`; `fixed T[]` is the type in a bare position; `fixed` before a
non-slice type there, `stack` on a return and `fixed` on a cast target are PARSE-013; a plain binding of the view is the
grouped type `(fixed uint8[]):v` (found building: a `fixed` FIELD is construction-only, so `view_escape.npk`'s re-assigned
field needed the group). (3) The production rule: a view of `fixed` storage is produced as the read-only view — a `fixed`
binding's own type, a range of a fixed place, a slice member read off a fixed place, and `string_bytes` (S-133 → D-351:
always). (4) The rights are read by `fits` (one way), by `place_fixed`'s index arm (the base's type), by the signature
comparison (by id), by `unify_into` through the element, by `type_subst`, and spelled by `type_display`; no cast between the
two views is admitted in either spelling; `argv`, `environ()`, `#wild_slice` and `Reader.read`'s `dest` stay plain. (5)
Nothing moves in the emitter (`emit_fit` passes the pair through; one representation), the floor, the encoder's theory
(a length term by kind; the sorts by Int/Bool/FloatingPoint), the escape, alias and bindings analyses, the derive generator,
the channels, `npkg`, the snapshot.

**What building it changed from the plan.** The two double reports (a write or an address into a call result reported
the value's TYPE-024 and then the view's TYPE-086/TYPE-071: `require_place` and `require_place_operand` answer false and
their callers stop); the tree's own readers of the bridge — seven sites in the prelude, the escape analysis and the alias
analysis, and fourteen test files — re-spelled `fixed uint8[]`; a seventh face of DEF-246 (a string LITERAL's bytes
written through the bridge fault too); the emission comparison's instrument, `tidnorm.py`, because interning the prelude's
`fixed uint8[]` ahead of every program's own types moves `@"npk.drop.<tid>"` and `@"npk.vacant.<tid>"` by one in every
emission's text while no artifact changes.

**The cost of D-351, measured on the libraries before the landing.** 1,065 files move, every one TYPE-007 at a reader
binding the bridge's view into a plain `uint8[]` slot: nitpick-fuzz 1,013 (956 generated cells of one idiom, `uint8[]:bs =
string_bytes(v);`, 43 findings, 5 known, 9 m10/m11 programs; a rejection cell's intended code stands behind the TYPE-007
until it is re-spelled), nitpick-regex 36 (5 source files, 13 sites: `core/bytes`, `hir/dump` ×5, `hir/repr`, `syntax/parse`
×4, `syntax/pattern_error` ×2; 31 tests), nitpick-time 16 (`src/core/bytes.npk`; 15 tests). Reported to the user with the
alternative (the read-only view only over a `fixed` place or a literal, which leaves a local string's bytes writable through
the bridge) before the advance notice; the ratified rule stands unless he revises.

### DEF-248 — LANDED 2026-10-08 (`nitpick-compiler_33`; worktree `wt/33a`, branch `33-def248` on landing 101's commit; landing 102; found by the probes of D-348 step (ii)'s planning)

**Where it started.** Planning D-348 step (ii) — `fixed T[]` as a type, `.internal/handoff_33/D348_II_PLAN.md` — probed every road
a `fixed` binding's promise travels, and one probe was the reference's own sentence: TYPE_REFERENCE §26 says "A parameter the
callee may not reassign" and that every position is `NITPICK-ASSIGN-002`; `func:g = int32(fixed int32:n) never fails { n = 2i32;
pass n; };` compiled on landing 100's compiler and answered 2 (`.internal/handoff_33/probes_d348/probe_param.npk`).

**The defect.** The bindings analysis's assignment arm (`assign_assign`, bindings.npk) handled a `DeclParamDecl` target with
`state_mark` alone, under a comment that said a parameter carries no `fixed` — false since 0.7.3, when `p_qualifiers` began
reading a parameter's leading qualifiers into `param_quals` (`place_fixed` reads the bit for an address and a part write,
TYPE-071 and TYPE-086). The whole-binding write was asked of nothing: a `fixed` parameter was a plain one to every assignment,
the compound form included (one `StmtAssignStmt` kind carries both).

**The fix.** The arm reads `param_quals & QUAL_FIXED` and reports ASSIGN-002 — "a `fixed` parameter is written by its caller and
never by its body (TYPE_REFERENCE §26): this assignment is the second write; drop `fixed` if the parameter is meant to change" —
before `state_mark`. No flow analysis, as for a `fixed` field: a parameter's one write is the call, so every assignment in the
body is the second. A plain and a `move` parameter keep DEF-124's re-assignment.

**The tests.** `tests/analysis/rejection/fixed_param_assign.npk`: five sites — a whole-binding assignment, one whose value reads the
parameter first, a compound assignment, a `fixed uint8[]` view parameter re-pointed (its bytes are D-348 (i)'s TYPE-086; its header
is the binding's), and the local twin (D-287's rule as it stood: the control that one sentence covers both positions) — beside the
controls (a plain parameter assigned, `fixed` parameters read and passed on, a `move` parameter re-initialised after its move); the
header measured after the file had its final line count: ASSIGN-002 at 28, 35, 41, 47 and 55, nothing else (an analysis rejection
file needs the suite's complete `failsafe`, or REACH-001 joins the set). DEF-124's `move_param_twice.npk` and
`move_param_reinit.npk` answer as before.

**Registered with it** (OPEN_DECISIONS §4). **DEF-246** — a plain view minted from or copied out of `fixed` storage writes it, SIX
faces measured on landing 100's compiler, two of them `MachineFault` in safe code (`string_bytes` of a `fixed` module string; a
`fixed` module array ranged), three silent (a `fixed` local array ranged; a view copied out of a `fixed` aggregate; a `fixed` view
assigned to a plain binding), and an impl of `Writer` that drops the trait's `fixed` on `wsrc`, accepted (`same_signature` compares
types; `fixed` is on the `DeclParamDecl`) and writing the caller's bytes through the bound — D-348 step (ii) closes all six by
construction; the plan is `.internal/handoff_33/D348_II_PLAN.md`; its questions S-132 (the spelling) and S-133 (the bridge) the user settled the same day as D-350 and D-351 ("your recommendation for those questions looks fine to me."), recorded by this landing; built as landing 103.
**DEF-247** — `fixed` and `stack` as RETURN qualifiers are accepted and read by nothing (`DECL_RET_FIXED`, `DECL_RET_STACK`
dormant; 0 uses anywhere); step (ii)'s landing refuses them.

**The measurements** (base = landing 101's binaries, `8af8ddd`). THE SWEEP under landing 101's checker and this one over 5,097
files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 5 sites appeared (the four parameter sites of `fixed_param_assign.npk` (its fifth, the local twin, is the rule as it stood and the base reports it too); and the fuzzer's own CLAIM program for this rule, nitpick-fuzz's `m11/programs/ty1873.npk` (its header: "claim: A fixed parameter may not be reassigned: ASSIGN-002; expect: refuse; wrong: accepted" -- the corpus had DEF-248 recorded as a wrong acceptance, and it refuses as expected now)),
0 vanished — 3,105 → 3,110; per code NITPICK-ASSIGN-002 25 -> 30. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 561 programs under both
compilers, 561 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by both. The
libraries and applications: 3,995 programs, 2,448 byte-identical, 1,546 refused by both, 0 different,
1 newly refused. D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The verify suite's
problem text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS,
0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 378 programs at both legs, 369 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:`
fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's 34 and
the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree — 7,063 obligations (3,424 discharged, 2,728 open, 0 budget, 906 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding
itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations` 6,767 rows before and 6,767 after,
6,583 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell;
the 184 rows not shared are RE-KEYED -- the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests are EQUAL (0 added, 0 removed), the problem texts of the rows after the edited arm in bindings.npk moving with their node ids; the floor's 388 unmoved. The in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A REFUSAL ADDED: `NITPICK-ASSIGN-002` at an assignment to a `fixed` parameter — the rule the
reference stated; no file of the tree, the libraries or the applications is refused anew but the new test and the fuzzer's `ty1873.npk`, its own claim program for this rule, which expected the refusal. No
emission moves for a program that compiles on both compilers; no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced

### D-348 STEP (ii) — LANDED 2026-10-08 (`nitpick-compiler_33`; worktree `wt/33b`, branch `33-d348ii` on landing 102's commit; landing 103; D-350 and D-351, the user's ratification of S-132 and S-133; DEF-246 and DEF-247 FIXED)

**Where it started.** D-348 (the user, 2026-10-08: "your recommendations are fine with me") made D-074's promise true in two
steps: (i) the write through a `fixed` slice refused (landing 98), (ii) `fixed T[]` a TYPE a plain view converts to and never
back. Planned first (`.internal/handoff_33/D348_II_PLAN.md`, §2.10e of this file as it landed): the probes measured the hole's SIX
faces (DEF-246 -- two `MachineFault`s in safe code, three silent writes, an impl of `Writer` dropping the trait's `fixed`), two
dormant return qualifiers (DEF-247) and a reassignable `fixed` parameter (DEF-248, landing 102); the user settled the spelling
(S-132: R1) and the bridge (S-133: always) on 2026-10-08 -- "your recommendation for those questions looks fine to me." --
recorded as D-350 and D-351 by landing 102.

**The type.** `fixed T[]` is `TY_SLICE` with its `b` slot set (`tt_slice_fixed`, `type_slice_fixed`): a distinct interned id,
so a plain `T[]` and a `fixed T[]` are two types to `fits` and to a signature comparison, while every kind-keyed walker (the 44
sites) keeps its answer. `fits` admits the plain view into the fixed one and never the reverse (the one admission beside
D-099's wrap that changes no bits; `fixed_view_into_plain` gives the three mismatch reporters the rule's sentence); `place_fixed`
reads the base's TYPE, so a write through a read-only view that reached a plain slot by type alone -- a `List<fixed uint8[]>`
element, a generic cell's field, a function's `fixed uint8[]` result -- is TYPE-086 and an address of its element TYPE-071; the
trait comparison sees the difference by id (TYPE-014, the sentence naming the slice parameter); `type_subst` keeps the rights;
the display spells them. A view is PRODUCED as the read-only one by a `fixed` binding of slice type, by ranging `fixed` storage
(an array binding, a module array, a slice held in a `fixed` aggregate, a `List` that is a fixed place), by reading a slice off a
`fixed` struct (`type_of_expr`'s wrapper, member access only), and by `string_bytes` (D-351: BUILTIN_REFERENCE's row is `string
→ fixed uint8[]`, the generated table and `builtin_text_type` carry the text). What stays plain: `argv`, `environ()`,
`#wild_slice`, `Reader.read`'s `dest`, every `T[]` a program writes.

**The spelling (D-350, R1).** At a declaration `fixed` is the binding's qualifier, as it was; a `fixed`-qualified binding of
slice type has its TYPE NODE marked (`ast_type_mark_fixed`, the one write onto a type node after its construction; the four
declaration parsers call `p_fixed_view_decl`), so the binding's type is the read-only view and the view leaves the binding with
its rights. In a bare type position -- a type argument, a function type's parameter, a return type, a grouped element -- the type
parser reads `fixed` before a slice type and marks it; before any other type it is `NITPICK-PARSE-013` at the keyword, the type
built all the same (D-240); `stack` on a return and `fixed` on a cast target are PARSE-013 too (DEF-247: `p_return_quals` reads
`wild`/`wildx` alone; `DECL_RET_STACK` and `DECL_RET_FIXED`, set and read by nothing, are deleted). A PLAIN binding of the
read-only view is spelled with the type grouped, `(fixed uint8[]):view` -- the qualifier loop sees no `fixed`, the type parser
does -- which is also how a struct field holds a re-assignable read-only view (`view_escape.npk`'s `Viewer`). A re-pointing
cursor over a range of itself is D-325's view-root freeze whatever the rights: a view is walked by an offset.

**Found building it.** (a) The tree's own readers of the bridge bound its view into plain slots: the prelude's `tw_write_all`,
the escape analysis's `paths_overlap` (and `header_field_disjoint_text`'s two parameters), the alias analysis's `decode_path`
(and `decode_step`, `decode_int`) -- seven re-spellings to `fixed uint8[]`, every one a read; and fourteen test files of the
tree (`builtin_sigs`, `views` (five sites: `tail`, `name_bytes`, `lit` return the view; `count_digits` takes it), `lent_dyn_swap`
twice, `view_escape` (`esc_view`, `esc_temp`, `stash`, the `Viewer` field), `view_root_moved_through_callee`, the three
`deref_store_*`, `move_param_reinit`, the two `pick_binding_*`, `pick_value_consume`, `view_in_frame`) -- the two rejection
files report exactly their headers again, the nine programs answer their expected exits. (b) TWO DOUBLE REPORTS of this
landing's own making, fixed before it landed: a write into a call result (`(raw f(v))[0i64] = b`) was TYPE-024 and then
TYPE-086, and `@(raw f(v))[0i64]` TYPE-024 and then TYPE-071 -- `require_place` and `require_place_operand` answer false after
TYPE-024 and their callers stop (D-240). (c) A SEVENTH face of DEF-246, measured sizing the alternative: a string LITERAL's
bytes written through the bridge fault too (`uint8[]:v = string_bytes("abc"); v[0i64] = 65u8;` exits 89 on landing 102's
compiler), since a literal lives in a constant global -- closed by D-351 with the others.

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/fixed_view_launder.npk` (fourteen sites: TYPE-007 x7 where a `fixed` view meets a plain

### DEF-249 — LANDED 2026-10-09 (`nitpick-compiler_34`; worktree `wt/34a`, branch `34-def249` on landing 103's commit; landing 104; the library seat's O-N41)

**Where it started.** The library seat (`nitpick-libs_14`, 2026-10-08) sent O-N41 from nitpick-time 0.3.2's planner at `5fbaf4a`
(`meta/roadmap/0.3/0.3.2_tools/facts.sh` §1.7): `exit may(argv.len);` with `func:may = int32(int64:k) { … fail EBoom; … }`
compiled, and `llc` refused the emitted module ("'%t3' defined with type '{ i32, i32 }' but expected 'i32'"), while the control
that assigns first, `int32:v = may(argv.len); exit v;`, was `NITPICK-TYPE-007` as it should be. `nitpick-compiler_33` reproduced
it the same evening at landing 102's compiler (`.internal/handoff_33/probes_on41/sh_exit.npk`, `sh_exitv.npk`) and registered
DEF-249 with the recommendation this landing carries out.

**The defect.** `check_exit` (type_stmt.npk) set the expectation — `e.expected = int32` — typed the operand under it and never
held the operand to it. A declaration's initialiser, a call's argument and a `pass` all ask `fits` after `type_of_expr` and report
through `te_assign_mismatch`; the `exit` statement asked nothing, so a `Result<int32>`, an `int64` or a `bool` went through to
the emitter, which lowers the operand as the `i32` the statement promises: the `Result`'s pair stood where an `i32` stands and
`llc` refused the module — a loud failure in the wrong phase (DEF-243's family), never a wrong answer. In `failsafe` the same.

**The fix.** `check_exit` keeps the operand's type and holds it to `int32` EXACTLY (`got != want`, the shape of a condition's
`!= t_bool`), reporting through `te_assign_mismatch` at the OPERAND's span — the one mismatch reporter, so the sentence is the
declaration's ("expected `int32`, found `Result<int32>`"; "found `int64`; there is no implicit widening, so write the cast";
"found `bool`"). Not `fits`: no conversion enters an exit code (no `Optional` wrap, no `dyn`, no view), the emitter stores the
operand raw, and the in-process check `check_slot_sites_agree` holds every `fits` site to an `emit_fit` partner for exactly that
reason — the landing's first form asked `fits`, and the check's first run on the tree refused it ("a slot the emitter would store
raw"): the instrument, read, chose the predicate. ONE MISTAKE, ONE REPORT (D-240): a refused operand (`got == 0`) is already
reported; an `exit` outside `main`/`failsafe` keeps its own sentence (TYPE-010) and nothing else; an entry point declared with
another return type has TYPE-083/TYPE-044 at its signature, and the operand that follows that signature (`int64(…) { exit 0i64; }`)
is not reported a second time — the first form's sweep showed exactly those two second reports, in `main_sig_ret.npk` and
`failsafe_sig_ret.npk`, and the guard on `fn_success` removes them. No emitter change: the checker's check makes a non-`int32`
operand unreachable there.

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/exit_operand.npk`: four sites — a fallible call, `exit 5i64;`, `exit true;`, and
`exit may(1i64);` inside a `failsafe` arm — beside the controls (`exit (may(argv.len) ?! EBoom);`, `exit (may(argv.len) ?| 1i32);`,
a `never fails` callee's bare `int32`, a binding); the header measured after the file had its final line count under the new
checker (TYPE-007 at 37:37, 38:34, 39:34, 49:39, nothing else); landing 103's checker reports nothing on the file and nothing on
the finding's `sh_exit.npk`, and `sh_exitv.npk` answers TYPE-007 at the declaration on both. The D-240 controls are the suite's
own `main_sig_ret.npk` and `failsafe_sig_ret.npk` (an entry point returning `int64` and exiting with one: TYPE-083 and TYPE-044
alone, as their headers say) and a misplaced `exit may(k);` in a helper (TYPE-010 alone; measured by hand). CONTROL_REFERENCE §4.6
carries the dated note (`code` is an `int32`, held to it by the checker); OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-249 FIXED.

**The measurements** (base = landing 103's binaries, `7e91730`). THE SWEEP under landing 103's checker and this one over 5,109
files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 4 sites appeared (the four sites of `exit_operand.npk`),
0 vanished — 4,571 → 4,575; per code NITPICK-TYPE-007 1720 -> 1724. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 562 programs under both
compilers, 562 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by both. The
libraries and applications: 4,003 programs, 1,788 byte-identical, 2,215 refused by both, 0 different,
0 newly refused. D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The verify suite's
problem text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS,
0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 379 programs at both legs, 370 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:`
fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's 35 and
the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree — 7,065 obligations (3,424 discharged, 2,728 open, 0 budget, 908 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding
itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations` 6,769 rows before and 6,769 after,
6,662 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell;
the 107 rows not shared are RE-KEYED -- a type-id move: the problem texts of rows in 26 functions of `alias`, `ir_expr`, `ir_types`, `numeric`, `smt_encode` and `smt_writer` name a struct type by its interned id (`|npk.f.<TYPEID>.field|`, D-317; `float_round`'s result struct 26165 -> 26168), the compiler's edited body interning three types ahead of them, and the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests are EQUAL (0 added, 0 removed), per symbol; the floor's 388 unmoved. The in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A REFUSAL ADDED: `NITPICK-TYPE-007` at an `exit` whose operand is not an `int32` — a loud failure
moved from `llc` to the checker, where the sentence names the fix; no library or application program is refused anew. No emission moves for a program that
compiles on both compilers; no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in advance (NOTICES F46).

**The landing's own path, for the record.** Built and measured by `nitpick-compiler_34` in `wt/34a` on landing 103's commit while
103's harness ran: the first form (`fits`) went through the chain and the re-record, the in-process slot-sites check refused it and
the sweep showed D-240's two second reports; the second form (the exact comparison, the two guards) went through the chain again,
the re-record after 103's harness ended (no z3 beside its verify stages), the harness on the final tree after that.

### DEF-159 — LANDED 2026-10-09 (`nitpick-compiler_34`; worktree `wt/34b`, branch `34-def159` on landing 104's commit; landing 105; found by 1.6.1e step 1's probes of DEF-120's own shape, 2026-09-26)

**Where it started.** 1.6.1e step 1's probes of DEF-120's own shape (2026-09-26) found that the shape DEF-120's record calls
legal — `string->:p = @x; string:t = move(x); (<-p) = v;` (a held `@` is D-286's plain party, and `move(x)` is not its
conflict) — LEAKS `v`: the move cleared `x`'s drop flag, the store put a live `v` into the vacated slot through `p`, and the
scope exit read the flag and dropped nothing; registered as DEF-159 with the recommendation this landing carries out, its own
landing after 1.6.1e's wrong answers. MEASURED FIRST on landing 104's compiler: the probe (a string per round, two thousand
rounds) 86,046 bytes live against 89 for one round; the committed twins (a string, a List and a struct per round) 192,094
against 190.

**The defect.** The drop flag (D-183, 1.2.3) is the scope-exit drop's test: one byte beside every owning local, set live at
initialisation, cleared by `move`, `pass` and `destroy`, loaded and branched on at every scope exit. Since DEF-120 (1.6.1d
step 3) every moved-out whole binding — and since DEF-158 every destroyed one — holds the type's canonical vacant value
(D-225), whose drop is a no-op, so the flag's one remaining job was the fast path: skip the call for a binding that owns
nothing. For a binding a pointer can reach, the fast path is what hid the value written back. `clear_root_flag` is the one
site that clears a local's flag; both its callers (`emit_move_out`, `destroy_vacate`) vacate the slot first; and nothing
re-arms the flag when `(<-p) = v` stores into the slot, because the emitter cannot know `p`'s target. The move analysis holds
`x` moved-from, so no read of `x` reaches the value either.

**The fix** (the registered recommendation). The function emitter remembers which owning bindings have had their address
taken — `FnEmitter.ltaken`, parallel to the local table (parameters included: they are its first entries) — marked by
`mark_root_taken` (ir_stmt.npk, beside `clear_root_flag`: the place's root through `place_root`, the binding through
`ident_slot`, a mark only where a flag exists) from the three address-producing lowerings: `emit_addr_op` (`@x`, `@x.f`,
`@l[i]`, and `$$i x`/`$$m x`, which build the same node pair), the pointer receiver of `emit_method_call` (the implicit
`@recv`, DEF-94's escape) and the awaited receiver of `emit_child_frame`. `emit_one_flagged_drop` drops a marked binding's
slot WITHOUT the flag test — one `call @"npk.drop.<tid>"(ptr slot)` where the load, the compare and the branch stood: a
vacant slot's drop is the no-op D-225 promises, a re-filled slot's is the drop the value was owed. A binding whose address
nobody took keeps the flag as its fast path. A mark is never cleared: the question is "could a pointer into this slot exist",
and once it could, it could until the scope ends.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/held_addr_store.npk`: six roads, exit 0 — a local string (the finding's shape), a
`move` parameter, a `List<int64>`, a struct with an owning field, a pointer-receiver call whose callee keeps `self` in a
registry parameter (the store lands through the registry after the move), and a coroutine whose store lands after a
suspension (the binding and its flag frame-resident); each road checks the moved body and the stored one.
`held_addr_churn.npk` / `held_addr_once.npk` under `tests/cost/held_addr.toml` (bound 2): a string, a List and a struct moved
out past a held address and stored over, two thousand rounds against one — 190 bytes live against 190 under this compiler,
192,094 against 190 under landing 104's. The suite's drop-sensitive programs (`overwrite_owned`, `pass_field`,
`move_param_reinit`, `destroy_field_churn`, `shared_arena_drop`, `list_drop`, `pick_binding_churn`, `template_owner`,
`dyn_cast_owner`, `deref_store_drop`, `view_in_frame`, `fd_ceiling`, `list_fds`, `chan_loop`, `arena_basic`,
`lent_dyn_swap`, `pick_value_consume`) answer their expected exits by hand; DEF-120's `deref_store_churn` keeps its 89.
MEMORY_REFERENCE §1.1c and DECISIONS (a dated note after D-254's DEF-120 note) carry the rule; OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-159
FIXED.

**The measurements** (base = landing 104's binaries, `085bc01`). THE SWEEP under landing 104's checker and this one over
5,112 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 0 sites appeared,
0 vanished — 4,575 → 4,575 (an emitter change: no site moves). THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 565
programs under both compilers, 518 byte-identical, 47 different in TEXT, 0 newly refused,
0 refused by both — and the 47 differing pairs READ through `meta/roadmap/1.6/tools/dropnorm.py`
(every scope-exit drop's flag test stripped from BOTH texts, temporaries and labels renumbered by first appearance):
47 of 47 identical after the strip and 0 not, 2,972 drops made unconditional in
all — each differing program holds an address-taken owning binding whose scope-exit drop lost its load, compare and branch,
and nothing else moves. The libraries and applications: 4,003 programs, 1,764 byte-identical, 2,215
refused by both, 24 different in text (24 identical after the strip, 0 not;
29 drops made unconditional), 0 newly refused. D-332's count over the five rejection suites:
0 mismatched files. The verify suite's problem text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both
compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 382 programs at both legs,
373 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The
frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's 35 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at
this tree — 7,067 obligations (3,424 discharged, 2,728 open, 0 budget, 910 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker;
`nitpick.obligations` 6,769 rows before and 6,771 after, 6,675 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among
shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell; the two rows added are the new accessors' (`fnem_mark_taken`'s and `fnem_local_taken_at`'s one `bounds` row each, `unencoded`: a `List` element's access with no length term -- E-4's residue, as every `List` access is), the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests differing by exactly those two, and the 94 rows not shared are RE-KEYED -- a type-id move: the problem texts of rows in 23 functions of `ir_expr`, `numeric`, `smt_encode` and `smt_writer` name a struct type by its interned id (`|npk.f.<TYPEID>.field|`, D-317), the emitter's new field and accessors interning ahead of them, the same rows per function verdict for verdict; the floor's 388 unmoved. The
in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** NO REFUSAL moves. A LEAK CLOSED: a value stored through a held `@x` after `move(x)` (or
after `x.destroy()`) is dropped at `x`'s scope exit. The emission of every program with an address-taken owning binding
moves in text — its scope-exit drop is one call where a load, a compare and a branch stood — and no program's answer does;
no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in advance (NOTICES F47).

**The landing's own path, for the record.** Built and measured by `nitpick-compiler_34` in `wt/34b` on landing 104's commit
while 104's harness ran (the fix, the six roads, the twins, `dropnorm.py`); the chain and the re-record after 104's harness
ended; the harness on the final tree.

### D-342's AND D-343's REFUSALS — LANDED 2026-10-09 (`nitpick-compiler_34`; worktree `wt/34c`, branch `34-d342-d343` on landing 105's commit; landing 106; the two refusals the user ratified 2026-10-01 as D-342 and D-343, S-122 and S-123)

**Where it started.** Two of the eight questions the user ratified in one sentence on 2026-10-01 ("all the recommendations
look fine to me. lets go with those.") were refusals the checker did not yet make: **D-342 R1** — an enum values EVERY
variant or NONE, since `{ A = 5i32; B; }` makes `B` 1 (its position) where a reader from C or Rust expects 6, the look-alike
trap the language's renaming rule exists to remove — and **D-343** — a macro parameter that only names an emitted declaration
is refused at the macro's declaration, since `macro:m = (N) { func:N = …; };` emitted a function literally called `N` with
the argument silently dropped (MACRO_REFERENCE §10's open question since 0.6.0). Landing 85 (DEF-193) had built TYPE-093's
three interim rules and step 3a (DEF-189) MACRO-011's two, each leaving its question open; the 1.6 README carried the pair as
"D-342's and D-343's refusals" at the head of the queue after DEF-159. MEASURED FIRST on landing 105's checker: `enum:Mixed =
{ First; Second; Tenth = 10i32; }` accepted (`enum_values.npk`'s own control); `macro:emitted_unused = (F) { func:F = … };`
accepted (`param_misplaced.npk`'s own control, and the listener's `mc0388`, which pins the old behaviour as running).

**The two rules.** D-342: `check_enum_values` (type_stmt.npk) walks the members once before the tag table; the first variant
whose spelling (valued or not) differs from the first variant's is `NITPICK-TYPE-093` with its own sentence ("has no explicit
value where the variants before it have one" or the converse; "an enum values every variant or none (D-342) — an unvalued
variant's tag is its POSITION, which a reader beside explicit values takes for the previous value plus one"), once per enum;
a mixed enum's unvalued variants then take no part in the tag table, because the position they would get is the mistake
already reported (D-240) — so `{ A; B = 0i32; }` and `{ A = 1i32; B; }`, which landing 85 reported as collisions, are
reported as the mix. The valued variants keep every other check. An enum with a payload variant can value none (a payload
variant takes no value: `Some(T) = 4i32` does not parse). D-343: `check_macro_params` (expand.npk) reports MACRO-011 for a
parameter the body DECLARES, used or not — `bad_decl = u.declared` where it was `u.declared && u.used` — the declared-and-used
sentence as before and a new one for the declared-only shape ("a declaration's name is never substituted, so the argument
would be dropped and the declaration literally called `N` (D-343): a macro cannot name what it emits from an argument"), at
the declaration, once per parameter.

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/enum_values.npk`: `Mixed` moves from the controls into the refused section (refused at
`Tenth`), `ByPosition` and `PositionLater` report the mix at `B` where they reported the collision; thirteen sites, the header
regenerated to a fixed point from the checker's own report (`.internal/handoff_34/tools/header_from_check.py`, which iterates
because a header that grows moves every site's line). `tests/expansion/rejection/param_misplaced.npk`: the four `*_unused`
shapes (a local, an emitted function, a generic parameter, a local beside `#caller(N)`) are refused; twenty sites, the same
regeneration; its comments say the question is settled. `tests/frontend/expansion_output.npk`'s two cases that pinned the
open question (`mk`, emitting `func:N`; `esc`, declaring `N` beside `#caller(N)`) expect one MACRO-011 each and no emitted
`N` — the chain's first frontend run found them (exit 109) and they were read, not loosened. `tests/backend/programs/enums_pick.npk`'s `Flat` values every variant
(`A = 0i32; B = 1i32; Late = 9i32;` — the same tags; exit 0). `tests/grammar/whole_grammar.npk`'s `Net` keeps its mixed
spelling: the file is parse-only by design and the checker never reaches its enum (its imports resolve to nothing first);
the grammar is unchanged — a mix parses and the CHECKER refuses it. A scan of the tree, the libraries and the applications
for enums mixing valued and unvalued variants found the three test enums and `Flat`; the two library hits (`tr0266`,
`tr0554`) are `Opt<T> = { Some(T); None; }`, unvalued. TYPE_REFERENCE's variant-value section gains the mix bullet (S-122
settled), MACRO_REFERENCE §3 the declared-name bullet and §10 the settled note, DECISIONS the two landing notes,
OPEN_DECISIONS the two rows' landing.

**The measurements** (base = landing 105's binaries, `a6b6edf`). THE SWEEP under landing 105's checker and this one over
5,112 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories; both checkers over the same files,

### DEF-243 — LANDED 2026-10-09 (built by `nitpick-compiler_34`, recorded and landed by `nitpick-compiler_35`; worktree `wt/34d`, branch `34-def243` on landing 106's commit; landing 107; the library seat's O-N40 item 1)

**Where it started.** nitpick-time 0.3.1's planning measured the shape before writing one: `func int64() never fails:f =
mono_now; int64:t = raw f();` is `NITPICK-EMIT-002` at the initialiser, and `raw call_it(mono_now)` with `call_it =
int64(func int64() never fails:g) never fails { pass raw g(); }` is EMIT-002 at the argument — "the emitter could not lower this,
although the frontend accepted it; a defect in the compiler" — where the same two shapes over an ordinary function compile and
exit 0. `nitpick-compiler_32` registered it as DEF-243 at landing 99 (O-N40 item 1) with the listener's own recommendation, the
frontend's refusal; it waited behind the wrong answers. MEASURED FIRST on landing 106's compiler: EMIT-002 at 4:34 and 5:27, the
control over `seven` exit 0 (`scratchpad/def243/bv1.npk`, `bv2.npk`, `bv_ctl.npk`).

**The defect.** A bare builtin's name resolves to no symbol — correct (AST_REFERENCE 3.3): its signature arrives with call
checking, from the generated table (D-201), which types a builtin's CALLS and says nothing about the bare name. `type_ident`
returned 0 for it — the type that means "refused and already reported" (D-240) — with nothing reported, so a slot asked nothing
more and the name passed into an initialiser, an argument, a struct literal's field, a `pass` or an assignment, and the emitter,
which holds a builtin as its intrinsic (`ir_runtime.npk`'s generated rows) and has no symbol to hand out, died by name.

**The fix** (the registered recommendation). `type_ident` (type_expr.npk) refuses an identifier that has no symbol and a
builtin's spelling: `NITPICK-TYPE-054` at the name — "`mono_now` is a builtin, and a builtin is called, never named as a value
(D-201, D-294): its calls are typed by its own rules and the emitter has no symbol for it to stand in for — write a function
that calls it and name that" — and returns the 0 that now means what it says. A call's callee must not come through it:
`type_call` (type_members.npk) typed the callee with `type_of_expr` first, so the first form reported every builtin CALL in the
prelude (`string_concat`, `alloc_managed`, `ralloc` at their call sites) and the test's own `mono_now()` control; `type_call`
reads a bare builtin callee by its spelling now and hands only a non-builtin callee to the identifier typer — the builtin table
types the call as before. D-294 makes a builtin's name the compiler's; a value nothing can call through the type system's one
path is a wrong shape, not a missing lowering (the alternative, a thunk per builtin so named, is the feature nobody asked for).

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/builtin_as_value.npk`: five sites — an initialiser, an argument, a struct literal's field,
a `pass` from a function returning a function type, an assignment — beside the controls (an ordinary function in every position;
the builtin CALLED), the header regenerated to a fixed point from the checker's report (TYPE-054 at 29:34, 33:22, 36:27, 40:10,
44:9, nothing else); landing 106's checker reports nothing on the file. The listener's two probes refuse at the name and its
control compiles and exits 0; the prelude-heavy programs (`deref_store_churn`, `dyn_slots`, `text_roundtrip`) are clean.
BUILTIN_REFERENCE's D-294 paragraph and DECISIONS (a dated note on D-294) carry the rule; OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-243 FIXED;
the 1.6 README's Remaining list keeps DEF-244 alone of the O-N40 pair.

**The measurements** (base = landing 106's binaries, `42ecf29`). THE SWEEP under landing 106's checker and this one over 5,113
files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 5 sites appeared (the five sites of `builtin_as_value.npk`),
0 vanished — 4,182 → 4,187; per code NITPICK-TYPE-054 8 -> 13. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 565 programs under both
compilers, 565 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by both. The
libraries and applications: 4,003 programs, 1,844 byte-identical, 2,159 refused by both, 0 different,
0 newly refused. D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The verify suite's
problem text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS,
0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 382 programs at both legs, 373 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:`
fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's 35 and
the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree — 7,067 obligations (3,424 discharged, 2,728 open, 0 budget, 910 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding
itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations` 6,771 rows before and 6,771 after,
6,662 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell;
the 109 rows not shared are RE-KEYED -- a type-id move: the problem texts of rows in 27 functions of `alias`, `ir_expr`, `ir_types`, `numeric`, `smt_encode`, `smt_writer` and `type_members` name a struct type by its interned id (`|npk.f.<TYPEID>.field|`, D-317), the edited checker interning a type ahead of them, and the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests are EQUAL (0 added, 0 removed), per symbol; the floor's 388 unmoved. program that compiles on both compilers; no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in advance (NOTICES F49).

### DEF-202 — LANDED 2026-10-09 (`nitpick-compiler_35`; worktree `wt/35a`, branch `35-def202` on landing 107's commit; landing 108; found by landing 87's probes)

**Where it started.** Landing 87 (DEF-179: a module constant of every literal family) probed the forms D-165 lists for a
module binding's initialiser and found one refused: "a reference to another module-level binding" held for a scalar and a
string -- `fixed flt64:TAU = PI;` -- while `fixed Pt:Q = P;` over a struct, `fixed int32[3]:B = A;` over an array and
`fixed int32?:O2 = O;` over a sentinel-initialised `Optional` were each `NITPICK-TYPE-035`, in the sentence that lists the
reference as legal ("a literal, a sentinel, a struct or array of constants, another module binding, or `comptime(…)`").
Registered 2026-10-01 as DEF-202 -- refused, not wrong -- with the recommendation this landing builds. MEASURED FIRST on
landing 107's compiler: the three shapes TYPE-035 at 11:1, 13:1 and 15:1 of the probe, the scalar reference beside them
compiling (`scratchpad/def202/mj1.npk`); a reference cycle RESOLVE-006 at both lines before the checker runs (`cyc.npk`); a
`fixed` module binding without an initialiser ASSIGN-001 at its own line (`vac.npk`).

**The defect.** The module-constant gate (`const_init_verdict`, type_stmt.npk) walks a struct or an array LITERAL member
by member, admits a sentinel, and hands everything else to the folder -- and the folder holds a scalar's and a string's
value and nothing else, so a name standing for a struct, an array or a sentinel folded to nothing and the gate said "not
a constant". The global renderer (`emit_global_const_into`, ir_expr.npk) has the same shape -- the literal arms, the
sentinel arm, then the folder -- so the same name was unrenderable there. Both asked the folder for a VALUE, and the
walk that says what a name STANDS FOR (the recorded symbol, a declaration, a module binding, `fixed`) was a private step
of the folder's identifier arm (`fold_ident`, type_resolve.npk).

**The fix** (the registered recommendation). That walk is one `pub` function, `fixed_global_sym` (type_resolve.npk):
which `fixed` module binding an identifier names, by the resolver's recorded symbol (never the spelling, DEF-196), or a
name in a type position by lookup; `fold_ident` reads it as before. The gate asks it where the folder answers nothing: a
reference to a `fixed` module binding the folder cannot hold is a constant -- the TARGET'S own declaration answers for
its initialiser (every module binding is checked; a cycle is RESOLVE-006 before the checker runs), so D-240's one report
stands at the binding that holds the mistake and never at its reader. The renderer asks it where the folder answers
nothing: the name is followed to the target's initialiser and rendered in the target's home scope (where its field names
and its own references resolve, D-137), as `emit_one_global` renders the target itself -- LLVM has no constant that is
another global's VALUE, so the reader's constant carries a copy; a target declared without an initialiser is
`zeroinitializer` for totality (ASSIGN-001 refuses it at its own line). On both sides the folder's own answer comes
FIRST, so every initialiser it folded before -- a scalar's, a string's, a float's -- is checked and written exactly as it
was: the emission comparison's zero.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/module_ref.npk`: a struct, a chain of references (`Q2 = Q = P`), an array, a
sentinel-initialised `Optional`, a struct holding an array followed whole, an array of structs, a struct holding a string,
references INSIDE a literal's members (`Box{ at: P, dims: A }`: the literal arms recurse into the new one), a reference
across an inline module (`use inner.{Far, FAR}; fixed Far:F2 = FAR;`: the target's home scope), and the scalar, float and
string references as the controls that compiled before -- twenty-one checks, each constant compared where it is read,
exit 0 at both legs; landing 107's compiler refuses the file at every aggregate reference (9 TYPE-035).
TYPE_REFERENCE's initialiser table gains the reference in its struct/array and `Optional`/pointer rows; DECISIONS carries
a dated note on D-165; OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-202 FIXED; the 1.6 README's Remaining list drops it.

**The measurements** (base = landing 107's binaries, `508d7c5`). THE SWEEP under landing 107's checker and this one over
5,114 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 9 sites vanished
(the 9 TYPE-035 sites of `module_ref.npk`, one per aggregate reference), 0 appeared -- 4,116 → 4,107; per code NITPICK-TYPE-035 23 -> 14. THE EMISSION
COMPARISON: 566 programs under both compilers, 565 byte-identical, 0 different, 0
newly refused, 0 refused by both, and one refused by the base alone -- the landing's own `module_ref.npk`.
The libraries and applications: 4,003 programs, 1,844 byte-identical, 2,159 refused by both,
0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by the base alone.
D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The verify suite's problem text:
133 of 133 files byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS, 0
FAIL. The program suite by hand: 383 programs at both legs, 374 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:`
fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's
35 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree — 7,067 obligations (3,424 discharged, 2,728 open, 0 budget, 910 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified
compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations` 6,771 rows before
and 6,771 after, 6,538 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind)
discharged counts fell; the 233 rows not shared are RE-KEYED -- a type-id move: the problem texts of rows in 46 functions of `alias`, `bindings`, `derive_gen`, `escapes`, `ir_expr`, `ir_types`, `lexer`, `numeric`, `operators`, `pipeline`, `resolve_path`, `smt_encode`, `smt_writer`, `token` and `type_resolve` name a struct type by its interned id (`|npk.f.<TYPEID>.field|`, D-317), the edited resolver interning a type ahead of them, and the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests are EQUAL (0 added, 0 removed), per symbol; the floor's 388 unmoved. The in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A REFUSAL REMOVED: a module binding initialised from another `fixed` module binding of a
struct, an array or an `Optional` type compiles, where it was `NITPICK-TYPE-035`; no library or application program moves. No emission
moves for a program that compiled before; no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in advance
(NOTICES F50).

### DEF-224 — LANDED 2026-10-09 under D-352 (`nitpick-compiler_35`; worktree `wt/35b`, branch `35-def224` on landing 108's commit; landing 109; the library listener's `probe02a`, registered 2026-10-01 at landing 92)

**Where it started.** nitpick-posix's PX-010 makes `failsafe` a macro: sixty utilities each carrying a hand-written exhaustive
`pick` is twelve hundred lines that must stay in sync, and REACH-002 checks exhaustiveness in `failsafe`'s own body, so a
shared helper FUNCTION discharges nothing. The listener's `probe02a_failsafe_macro.npk` asked whether `#posix_failsafe(e);`
in `failsafe`'s body could supply the pick, and stated its own doubt: a statement-position expansion "becomes a block holding
the statements" (MACRO_REFERENCE §5), and if the reach analysis looks for the pick among `failsafe`'s own statements it will
not see one. Landing 92's sweep reached the question (until D-340 the probe stopped earlier, RESOLVE-002 at the argument) and
registered it as DEF-224, "to be READ before it is called a defect": whether `failsafe`'s pick may stand inside a block at
all, against D-179's exhaustive-`failsafe` rule and REACH-002's reading of the arms. MEASURED FIRST on landing 108's
compiler: `probe02a` (the macro) and `probe02e` (a hand-written bare block) `NITPICK-REACH-001`, and a pick inside
`if (ALWAYS)` REACH-001 (`scratchpad/def224/`).

**The reading, and the decision.** D-179's words are that `failsafe` "must contain a `pick` over its `Error` parameter
whose named arms cover the computed set"; `reach_settle` read "contain" as "among the body's own statements" (one loop over
the body block, the last pick met). What the rule needs is that every trap REACH the pick: a bare block runs unconditionally
where it stands, so a pick inside one is every trap's, and a statement macro's expansion is a bare block (expand.npk builds
it as one); a pick inside a conditional, a loop or an arm is not every trap's. Put to the user 2026-10-09 with that
recommendation -- through bare blocks at any depth, never through an `if`, a `when`, a loop or another pick's arm; one pick,
as now; the alternative a generated `failsafe` per utility -- and ratified the same day as **D-352**, in his words: the two
goals are a `failsafe` a large project importing many libraries can keep and no error falling through the cracks
unacknowledged, and the reading "attempts to strike the best balance of those goals". His broader question (arm-level
composition across many libraries' error sets, which no macro position offers) is S-134, registered and not acted on.

**The fix.** `failsafe_pick_in` (reach.npk): the last pick in a depth-first walk of the body through `StmtBlockStmt`
children only, `fuel` the tree's own bound (`REACH_DEPTH`; the parser refuses a deeper tree); `reach_settle` calls it where
it scanned the body's statements. Every other statement kind that holds a block -- a conditional's, a loop's, an arm's, a
`defer`'s -- is not entered, so REACH-001 stands as before for a pick inside one. The arm contract (REACH-002) is asked of
the pick wherever it was found; MACRO-009's note points a report inside an expansion into the macro body.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/failsafe_pick_macro.npk` (the macro supplies the pick; an index the compiler cannot
fold reaches the runtime bounds check, `OutOfBounds` answers 70; the full arm set) and `failsafe_pick_block.npk` (the same
pick hand-written in a bare block nested twice: the walk's, not the expander's) -- exit 70 at both legs, each REACH-001 on
landing 108's compiler; `tests/analysis/rejection/failsafe_cond_pick.npk` (the pick inside `if (ALWAYS)`: REACH-001 still --
the rule is syntactic, as the rest of the reach analysis is). The listener's `probe02a` (the macro with a parameter), `probe02d` (the macro's
`#caller(e)` form) and `probe02e` (the hand-written bare block) pass the pick search and are REACH-002 for `StackExhausted`
and `MachineFault`, the two arms their set lacks since 1.5.8; a `probe02f`-shaped control (the pick inside a conditional)
stays REACH-001. DECISIONS carries D-352 (the user's words) and a dated note on D-179; MACRO_REFERENCE §5's expansion
row says the block is bare and may carry the pick; OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-224 FIXED and registers S-134; the 1.6 README's
Remaining list drops DEF-224.

**The measurements** (base = landing 108's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 108's checker and this one over 5,117 files
(the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 5 sites vanished (the two REACH-001 of `failsafe_pick_macro.npk` and `failsafe_pick_block.npk`; and the REACH-001 of the listener's `probe02a_failsafe_macro.npk`, `probe02d_caller_body.npk` and `probe02e_block_nested.npk` (nitpick-posix), each passing the pick search now),
8 appeared (the same three probes reaching REACH-002 for `StackExhausted` and `MachineFault`, two sites each -- their arm set predates 1.5.8 -- and the MACRO-009 note under the two macro forms, pointing the report into the macro body) -- 4,190 → 4,193; per code NITPICK-REACH-001 10 -> 5; NITPICK-REACH-002 38 -> 44; note:NITPICK-MACRO-009 31 -> 33.
