### DEF-242 — LANDED 2026-10-17 (`nitpick-compiler_39`; worktree `wt/39g`, branch `39-def242` on landing 125's commit; landing 126; registered at landing 97; first after 125 by the author's reorder of 2026-10-16 at 23:35:51 -- Q37, through `management_1`: "nice! for q37, yes i am fine with that.")

**Where it started.** Landing 97's emission comparison (DEF-231, the frac section) showed 561 of 561 tree programs and 2,435 of 2,435 library programs different in text by the site table's renumbering alone, and `nitpick-compiler_32` registered DEF-242 reading it: D-179's error-origin site table (`@npk.sitep.N`, `@npk.site.lines`, `@npk.site.paths`; read by `site_path`/`site_line` when a `failsafe` names a trap's origin) numbered the prelude's sites first and the program's after them, and every emission carried every prelude site -- the tables were written BEFORE D-262's trim, so they were roots the trim kept whole: some 290 `prelude.npk` rows and a 300-entry pair of tables in a program that calls none of those bodies -- so a prelude edit moved EVERY program's text (landing 97: 561 of 561 tree programs and 2,435 of 2,435 library programs by the renumbering alone), the library side's per-program digests moved across every such re-pin (F39), and an emission comparison had to be canonicalised (`meta/roadmap/1.6/tools/sitenorm.py`) before it said anything. The plan is `.internal/handoff_35/D242_PLAN.md`.

**What the code did.** `irw_site(w, path, line)` (ir_writer.npk) is the one registration -- a (path, line) row deduplicated in first-sight order, the id `i + 1` (0 the runtime's reserved site) -- and every consumer wrote the id inline through `irw_line` as one of two call shapes; `emit_all` wrote the tables (`emit_site_tables`) BEFORE it trimmed the items, so the tables were roots the trim kept whole, every site ever registered in every program.

**The change.** `emit_all` (emit_program.npk) trims the items FIRST (`irw_trim_items`), then `irw_renumber_sites` (ir_writer.npk, new) scans the kept text for the two call shapes `call void @npk_chain_reset(i32 N)` / `call void @npk_chain_push(i32 N)` -- the whole surface of a site reference: every writer goes through `irw_line` (35 sites: ir_expr 24, ir_stmt 6, ir_rules 2, emit_program 2, ir_func 1), and a writer nobody anticipated is caught by the belt, never misnumbered in silence -- maps the referenced ids to dense new ids from 1 in ascending OLD id order (registration order, D-078: deterministic), rewrites them in the text and keeps the old ids in `IrWriter.site_keep`; `emit_site_tables` then writes `@npk.sitep.K`, `@npk.site.lines` and `@npk.site.paths` for the kept sites alone, each row's path and line read through its old id, the two lookups' bound the kept count + 1. The ids are the emission's own constants, so nothing changes at run time: a trap's origin prints the same path and line, the chain's semantics stand (reset at an origin, push at a relay), the runtime's reserved site 0 stays, no obligation row, guard or node id moves, the explorer's step counts are untouched (the tables are data). THE BELT, in both runners: `check_site_table_dense` (harness.py) and `ir_site_table_dense` (npkg/irscan.npk), asked of every module the compiler under test emits beside the prelude-trim belt (a program's emission, the verified build's, the compiler's own; never the snapshot's, the 1.5.2d rule) -- the set of ids the two call shapes reference equals {1..n} where n+1 is the lines table's length (row 0 the runtime's reserved site); a spare row is the renumbering not running, an id past the table a writer that spells a chain call another way; a module with no table and no call passes (the floor, the shim); each runner's self-check plants three cases (a dense table, a spare row, a call past the table), the harness's texts byte for byte.

**Found on the way.** FOUND BY THE LANDING'S OWN PROGRAM TEST AND FIXED WITH IT, DEF-263: the `npk_site_path` lookup `emit_site_tables` writes answered `{ ptr, len, len }` -- the string's third word, its `cap`, was the LENGTH, which is the ownership bit set (D-183: `cap == 0` is a body the value does not own) -- so `string:origin = site_path(chain_site(0i32));` in a `failsafe` freed the `@npk.sitep.N` constant at the scope's exit: `npk.drop` -> `npk_dalloc` -> `npk_heap_bad` -> `HeapBadRequest` inside `failsafe`, the re-entry exit 70 at both legs, on every compiler since D-179 landed the table (1.1.6). `origin_chain.npk`, the one program of the tree reading the chain, renders ids through `site_line` alone, and the three fuzz programs that call the lookups (`bi0263`, `bi0264`, `bi0265`) read site 0, whose answer is `zeroinitializer` (a view already), so the fault had no witness until the new test bound a real site's answer. The lookup writes `i64 0` as the third word now -- the answer is a VIEW of the table's constant, never dropped and never freed -- and BUILTIN_REFERENCE's row says so.

**Measured.** MEASURED FIRST on landing 123's compiler (wt/39g's first base; the checker is untouched, so the sweep cannot move): a floor-only probe (`wt/39g/.internal/probes/st_floor.npk`, thirteen `?!` sites of its own) emitted `@npk.site.lines = internal constant [307 x i32]`, 306 `@npk.sitep.` rows (293 of them `prelude.npk`) and thirteen chain calls (ids 294..306) in 59,224 bytes; under this compiler the same probe carries 14 rows, thirteen `@npk.sitep.` rows (its own file's) and the same thirteen calls (ids 1..13) in 25,864 bytes; the compiler's own emission 4,357 -> 4,092 rows (31,926,761 -> 31,895,403 bytes). Every `npk_chain_` line of both compilers' emissions that is not a `declare` and not one of the two call shapes is one of the compiler's own string constants (its emitter's text, `c"call void @npk_chain_"`), so the two shapes ARE the whole surface; the belt over the four emissions: the new two dense, the base's with 293 and 265 spare rows. `origin_chain.npk` (the tree's one reader of the chain, pinning two of its own lines) exits 42 at both legs under the new compiler; the whole program suite at both legs shows the nine `// argv:` fixture programs and nothing else.

**Tests.** `tests/backend/site_table.npk` (a writer-level unit: three registered sites, a text referencing the first and the third -- an origin and a relay -- and nothing referencing the second: the second is dropped, the third becomes 2, `site_keep` reads [1, 3], a `declare` without a number and a string constant spelling the call are untouched), `tests/backend/programs/site_table_rows.npk` (a prelude raise -- `list_pop` on an empty list -- reaches `failsafe`, which reads the origin back through `site_path`/`site_line` and walks the whole table: the kept prelude bodies' rows (`list_init<int32>` and `list_pop<int32>`: four at this writing), the file's own, nothing else, under forty rows where every compiler before 126 wrote some three hundred; exit 42 at both legs; landing 125's compiler exits 70 at both legs -- DEF-263's fault inside `failsafe`), and each runner's self-check's three planted cases (`site-table-dense-control`, `site-table-spare-row`, `site-table-past-end`).

The tool's first reading, before it learnt the three things this landing teaches it, left 9: seven pairs (`cast_wide`, `simd_compound`, `simd_ovf_add`, `simd_ovf_edge`, `simd_ovf_mul`, `tests/verify/overflow_lanes`, `tests/verify/simd_lanes`) whose `declare` lines of the late intrinsics (`llvm.*.with.overflow.*`, `llvm.ctlz.*`: `llctx_note_decl`'s block) and one blank line stand on the other side of the site tables now that the tables are written after the trim -- identical lines, moved, nothing else; `origin_chain.npk`, whose `npk_chain_site(i32 1)` the tool had read as a SITE id where the builtin takes a chain DEPTH; and `npkg/main.npk`, whose planted self-check texts SPELL a chain call inside a string constant the tool had rewritten. `sitenorm.py` reads `declare` lines as a set, skips blank lines, maps no id inside a module-level constant and leaves `npk_chain_site` alone now, and every pair is SAME -- the ids and the tables are the whole difference. ### D-339 — LANDED 2026-10-17 (built by `nitpick-compiler_39`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_40`; worktree `wt/39h`, branch `39-d339` on landing 126's commit; landing 127; 1.6.1e step 3c; the user's ratification of S-119 on 2026-10-01 as D-339; DEF-181 FIXED)

**Where it started.** DEF-181 (2026-09-30, step 3's probes): six holes of the lock-order proof and one over-restriction, each ACCEPTED or wrongly refused on every compiler since 0.5.6; S-119's floor; D-339 ratified the closing with "measured on the tree first". Planned execution-grade at `.internal/handoff_35/D339_PLAN.md`.

**Measured first.** The eight probes on landing 125's compiler (`wt/39g/.internal/probes/locks/`): `pa_hole` (1), `pa_hole_dyn` (the floor), `pd_self` (2), `pf_hold` (3), `pf_defer` (4), `p_sentinel` (6) ACCEPTED; `pn_helper` (the over-restriction) `NITPICK-LOCK-001` at 27:80; `pm_join` (5) compiling and exiting 21 (`DeadlineExceeded`) at both legs. The census over the three trees: `acquires` outside the compiler's tree in four fuzz programs only (`vf0829`, `vf0830`, `vf0831` refused as their headers expect; `as0034` in a comment), `acquires <=` in `vf0830` alone, a typed acquisition in 27 fuzz programs and in no library or application source.

**The rules.** (1) a direct call of an IMPLEMENTATION bounded `acquires <= N` is judged by the body's own summary, never by the bound (the bound is checked where the body is walked); (2) a function NAMED AS A VALUE reaches nothing -- `NITPICK-LOCK-002` at the name when its summary reaches any level, since a value is called from anywhere with anything held and a function type carries no clause (D-056's sentence for an undeclared dynamic call); (3) a binding raises the held level to the HIGHEST hold its initialiser may carry (`hold_max`, where `hold_min` had let a function that took and released 1 and returned holding 9 leave its caller believing it held 1); (4) a `defer` body is walked at ITS BLOCK'S END with the block's final held level -- D-207's order is join, defers, drops, so every later guard is still held when it runs; (5) a SPAWN (`drop` of an `async` call) is absorbed where it stands and judged at its block's end against the block's final held level, `NITPICK-LOCK-001` at the spawned call -- the parent joins the task holding that level and the child waits for a level at or below it (`pm_join`: exit 21, `DeadlineExceeded`, at both legs before this landing); (6) `2147483647` and `-2147483647`, the walk's own `none` values, are refused as levels -- `NITPICK-LOCK-003` in a clause (with a level outside `int32`, which the cast truncated before), `NITPICK-TYPE-016` in a lock type's level argument (`Mutex`, `RwLock`, `CondVar`, `Barrier`, `Channel`) -- from ONE authority (`LEVEL_RESERVED_HI`/`LO`, type_resolve.npk) the walk's sentinels read; THE FLOOR (D-339 (a)): a call that resolves to a TRAIT'S method bounded `acquires <= N` -- through a `dyn`, a bound `T: Trait`, `Trait.m(x)`, or a concrete receiver whose impl runs the default -- is legal only with NOTHING held, `NITPICK-LOCK-001` otherwise with the floor's own sentence, and the caller is FLOORLESS (propagated by the widening like a level: a call of IT while anything is held is refused the same way, naming the bounded method it reaches through); EXACT IS EXACT (D-339 (a)): every call of a method under an exact `acquires N` on the trait, or of an implementation of it, is judged at N, and an implementation whose body reaches BELOW N (or reaches through a `<=`-bounded method) is `NITPICK-LOCK-002` where it is written, as one reaching above N was -- so the contract IS the summary once the body is legal, and a refused body is reported once (D-240); and THE OVER-RESTRICTION: a binding raises the held level only when the hold is VISIBLE -- the callee declares an exact `acquires N` (its result is the lease), the callee is a bounded trait method, the initialiser is a typed acquisition, or the result's TYPE can carry a `Guard`/`RGuard` (directly or inside a struct, an enum, an `Optional`, a `Result`, an array: the layout bit `tt_hasguard`, ensured before it is read, D-227) -- so a helper that takes and releases inside and returns an `int32` may be bound twice in one block, and a function that hands a user-levelled lease on WRITES `acquires N`.

**The tests.** `tests/analysis/rejection/lock_floor.npk` (thirteen sites, five controls), `tests/types/rejection/lock_level_sentinel.npk` (two), `tests/backend/programs/lock_helper_twice.npk` (exit 3 at both legs; refused LOCK-001 by every compiler before), `lock_undeclared.npk` (case 6b re-spelled: the implementation below an exact 3 is LOCK-002, the direct call under 2 judged at the contract and silent), `chan_levels.npk` (case 8 re-spelled with a `Guard`: a clause-less wrapper's `Lease` is no visible hold -- the one prediction that failed, read as the rule's own consequence), `tests/accept/lock_levels.npk` (a helper bound twice, a `defer` written after its guards, a spawn whose guard ends in an inner block; the failsafe names `DeadlineExceeded`).

**The measurements**, all READ before the record. THE SWEEP under landing 126's checker and this one over 5,211 files: 14 sites appeared (the ten of `lock_floor.npk` the base did not report -- 66:11 hole 1 (judged by the body), 80:6 the floor, 92:13 its propagation through `via`, 112:5 and 158:34 LOCK-002 (`Shallow.open` below its exact 3; `reaches_1` named as a value), 184:36 the highest hold, 195:29 the `defer`, 217:15 the spawn, 239:36 and 240:45 the two sentinels (LOCK-003); its other three sites (a bound call holding 3, the two typed holds) both compilers report -- `lock_undeclared.npk`'s LOCK-002 at 152:5 (`Shallow.open` under `acquires 3`), `lock_level_sentinel.npk`'s two TYPE-016 (19:22, 25:25) and the fuzz corpus's `cc0427` (LOCK-001 at 23:16: its `Result<Guard<int32>>:late` bound in the block that joins `holder`, which acquires the same level 3)), 5 vanished (the base's own readings of the landing's files: `lock_floor.npk`'s two controls (120:16 `opens_under_two`, judged by the body's 1 where the contract's 3 governs now; 260:25 `twice`'s second binding, the over-restriction), `lock_undeclared.npk`'s 164:16 (case 6b's LOCK-001 at the direct call, judged at the contract now), `accept/lock_levels.npk`'s 138:25 (`twice`, the over-restriction) and the program `lock_helper_twice.npk`'s 25:25 (the over-restriction); the three re-spelled tree programs and `chan_levels.npk`'s re-spelled case 8 move nothing (both compilers accept the first, both report the second's 164:21)). THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 580 programs under both compilers, 579 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 1 refused by the base alone. The libraries and applications: 4,061 programs, 1,894 byte-identical, 2,166 refused by both, 0 different, 1 newly refused (`cc0427`, the fuzz corpus's expiry claim: its spelling, a `Result<Guard>` bound in the block that joins the child). The program suite by hand: 396 programs at both legs, 387 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's 36 and the emitter's 6 unit tests pass. The manifest: see the commit (every `src/` change re-keys it; the new walks' rows are read function by function).

**What changes for a program.** A call through a `<=`-bounded trait method while anything is held is refused, and so is a call of a function that makes one -- declare the trait method's level exactly, or take the guard after the call; an implementation below an exact `acquires N` is refused; a function value that acquires is refused at the name; a `defer` body and a spawn are judged at their block's end; a level equal to either sentinel is refused; and a helper that takes and releases a lock inside and returns a number may be bound twice in one block.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/buffer_index.npk` (exit 0 at both legs; every compiler before 128 refuses it), `buffer_index_oob.npk` and `buffer_range_oob.npk` (94), `tests/types/rejection/buffer_index_rules.npk` (five sites), `tests/analysis/rejection/wild_slice_tied.npk` (five sites, three controls), `tests/verify/bounds_buffer.npk` (3 `bounds` discharged), `index_pointer.npk`'s fifth site, `buffer_basic.npk`'s checked spellings.

**The measurements**, all READ before the record. THE SWEEP under landing 127's checker and this one over 5,217 files: 26 sites appeared (eighteen in nitpick-time, every one the belt's: `src/core/bytes.npk`'s `b.body = move(fresh);` at line 140 is BORROW-015 twice -- 140:6 the write to `b.body` under `from`'s view of `b.body.ptr`, 140:19 the `move(fresh)` under `into`'s view of `fresh.ptr` -- reported in the module and again in each of the seven files that reach it (`src/lib.npk`, `tests/conformance/import.npk`, `tests/probe/probe16e_sealed_reads.npk`, `tests/unit/bytes_growth.npk`, `tests/unit/bytes_put_int.npk`, `tests/unit/bytes_view_lifetime.npk`, `tests/unit/vec_at_pod.npk`), and `bytes_view_lifetime.npk`'s own two `bytes_push` calls after `bytes_view` (77:63, 107:63); the five sites of `wild_slice_tied.npk` (BORROW-015 at 31:5, 39:21, 56:7 and 65:15, BORROW-001 at 47:10); `buffer_index_rules.npk`'s TYPE-082 (36:56) and its `fixed` range's TYPE-007 (41:5) -- its TYPE-086 (24:5) and TYPE-085 (31:5) both checkers report, the base beside its "cannot be indexed"; and `index_pointer.npk`'s fifth TYPE-082 (28:56, the `buffer->` case)), 44 vanished (every one the base's reading of the landing's own files -- its TYPE-007 "cannot be indexed" or "cannot be ranged" at every buffer index and range of `buffer_index.npk` (20), `buffer_basic.npk`'s checked spellings (3), `buffer_index_oob.npk` (1), `buffer_range_oob.npk` (1), `bounds_buffer.npk` (3) and `wild_slice_tied.npk`'s inner-block control (1); `buffer_index_rules.npk`'s 11 (nine such, a second TYPE-007 at the `bool` index, and a TYPE-047 at `pass p[0i64]`, where the base passed the indexed BUFFER by value); and `index_pointer.npk`'s 4 (the new `buffers` case's TYPE-047 and TYPE-007 at 28:49, `fine`'s two at 34:11 and 34:40)). ### DEF-244 — LANDED 2026-10-17 (`nitpick-compiler_40`; worktree `wt/40b`, branch `40-def244` on landing 128's commit; landing 129; the library seat's O-N40 item 2, registered 2026-10-08 at landing 99)

**Where it started.** O-N40 (2026-10-08): the loader keyed a module by a path's SPELLING after a textual collapse of `.` and `..`, so a root named `a.npk` from its own directory and the same file imported back from a sibling directory as `../b/../a/a.npk` were two keys -- the file was read twice, its one error constant declared twice under one code (`NITPICK-RESOLVE-010`, "an FNV collision") and its `main` once as the root's and once as a module's (`NITPICK-RESOLVE-013`); named from the parent directory the same program loaded once. The row recommended the file's identity, decided at one place in the loader.

**Measured first.** The pair on landing 128's compiler (`wt/40a/.internal/probes/def244/`, and the same pair in a directory with no manifest above it): the three refusals from `a/` in both locations, none from the parent.

### DEF-245 and DEF-260 — LANDED 2026-10-18 (`nitpick-compiler_40`; worktree `wt/40c`, branch `40-def245` on landing 129's commit; landing 130; DEF-245 registered 2026-10-08 at landing 101, DEF-260 registered 2026-10-16 at landing 120 — the regex planner's observation; planned first at `.internal/handoff_35/DEF245_PLAN.md`)

**Where it started.** Landing 101's sweep (DEF-164) found a sixth face it did not fix: a declaration whose header fails was parsed on into its body, and the declaration-level recovery, stopping at `is_sync_point`'s STATEMENT keywords, left the body's `pass` and `}` to the module loop as two more declarations, "expected a type" each -- seven of the fuzz corpus's programs, two to four reports of one mistake. DEF-260 (2026-10-16): the recovery after a keyword-named field skipped the next field's report, because the broken field had been parsed to its own `;` and the recovery skipped from there to the next sync point.

**Measured first.** On landing 129's compiler (`wt/40c/.internal/probes/def245/measure_base.txt`): the seven fuzz programs as DEF-245's row says, line for line; three keyword-named fields two reports; every declaration kind with a block behind a broken header two to six reports.

**The change.** (a) The header bail: every declaration parser with a body, a member list or an initialiser tests `p_in_panic` after its header and returns its own kind with empty windows before the part that recovers on its own (`p_parse_func`'s comment is the rule; `p_parse_mod` the exception, since a member-less module node is a file import to the loader -- found by the kinds probe). (b) `p_recover_decl` stops at `is_decl_sync_point`'s set (no statement keyword, no `comptime`, no `#`) at bracket depth 0 only, consuming a `;`, leaving a `}`, stepping over a `)` or `]`; a recovery that begins right after a consumed `;` skips nothing (DEF-260, `p_prev_kind`). A macro's declaration body recovers per member; `p_parse_module` eats the `}`/`;` tail a declaration that reported leaves at the top level. The success path is untouched.

**The tests.** `parse_one_report.npk` +24 cases (the fuzz shapes, every kind, the controls, DEF-260's three fields and two error constants); the frontend's 37 units; the probes (`.internal/probes/def245/`): six of the seven fuzz programs one report, `tr0550` 4 -> 3 (DEF-265 registered: the statement recovery's artefact), the kinds one each, the keyword-named fields three.

**The measurements**, all READ before the record. THE SWEEP under landing 129's checker and this one over 5,218 files: 0 sites appeared (none: a header bail and a stopping recovery remove reports and add none, and no file of the three trees holds DEF-260's shape outside the unit), 20 vanished (every one a phantom in the fuzz corpus -- the seven programs DEF-245's row names, six more of the same family and one finding's probe, each file keeping its first report). THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 584 programs under both compilers, 584 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 refused by the base alone. The libraries and applications: 4,061 programs, 1,888 byte-identical, 2,173 refused by both, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by the base alone. The program suite by hand: 398 programs at both legs, 389 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The verify suite: 134 files PASS; the problem text of 134 of 134 files byte-identical under both compilers. The frontend's 37 and the emitter's 6 unit tests pass. The manifest: 7,268 obligations, 6,957 -> 6,966 rows, 6,831 shared, ZERO moved, ZERO fell; nine rows added and none moved or removed: 6 `overflow` in `p_recover_decl` (the bracket depth; 2 discharged, 4 open), 2 `terminate` in `p_parse_module` (the tail rule's loop, open) and 1 `overflow` in `p_prev_kind` (open); the 126 rows not shared are re-keyed, read on all 34 functions' texts (28 byte-identical with ids blanked, the six others the landing's own edits).

**What changes for a program.** A declaration whose header the parser refuses is one report, whatever block follows it; a keyword-named member's refusal no longer hides the next member's; nothing that parsed moves.

### DEF-265 AND DEF-271 — LANDED 2026-10-21 (built by `nitpick-compiler_40`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_41`; worktree `wt/40i`, branch `40-def265` on landing 138's commit; landing 139)

**Where it started.** Landing 130's measurement of the fuzz corpus's `tr0550`: after `Box < int32` failed inside a body, `main`'s body ended at a struct literal's `}` and every statement after it was a module-level "expected a type".

**Measured first.** On a WIP compiler of the queue and again on landing 138's (`wt/40i/.internal/probes/def265/`), read line by line: the expression failed at `int32` without consuming it, `p_recover` stopped there at once, the next statement failed at the same token (deduplicated), and the belt `p_sync` stepped over the literal's `{` and stopped at its `}`; `stmt_brace.npk` 2 reports, `lit_brace.npk` 3, `tr0550` 3, `as0594` 3, `ty1282` 2. DEF-271, found by this landing's first sweep (five fuzz programs gained a false PARSE-003): on landing 137's compiler and again on 138's (`wt/40i/.internal/probes/def271/`) a failed `if`, `while` or `loop` header inside `main` with a function after it four reports, the C `for` five, a lone `main` with the C `for` PARSE-003 at `main`'s `{`.

**The change.** `p_sync` counts `{`/`}` and stops at a sync point only at depth 0; `p_recover` skips a `{` that follows a name or a `>` whole (`p_skip_brace_group`); `p_parse_block` short of its `{` is an empty block, and what follows is the enclosing block's (DEF-271); and, from the chain's reading, a failed parenthesised header is skipped to its `)` (`p_recover_header`, for `if`, `while`, `when`, `for` and a `pick`'s selector, panic cleared so the body is the statement's own) and an arm's recovery is to the next arm (`p_recover_arm`; the arms without their `{` read nothing, as a headless body does).

**The tests.** `parse_one_report.npk`'s cases 150-174 (DEF-265's two shapes and two controls; DEF-271's five headers; and ten from the chain's reading: a removed macro pattern as an arm, a `for` binding with a qualifier, a `pick` whose selector fails, an `if` whose condition fails with an `else`).

The manifest: 7,299 obligations, 6,975 -> 6,997 rows, 6,855 shared, ZERO moved, ZERO fell; the 120 rows not shared on the old side (142 on the new) are 120 RE-KEYED (an id move, the edited source declaring symbols ahead of them -- read on all 35 functions' texts) and 22 ADDED, every one the landing's own and every one `open`: the five parser walks' guards -- `p_recover_arm`'s 5 `terminate` and 2 `overflow`, `p_recover_header`'s 2 and 4, `p_skip_brace_group`'s 2 and 2, `p_sync`'s 2 and 2 (its depth counter) and `p_recover`'s 1 `terminate` -- a loop measured by `p_left`, an uninterpreted function, and a depth counter bounded by the token count the solver cannot see; none of them a moved verdict (open 2,802 -> 2,824 exactly, discharged 3,236 and unencoded 932 unmoved), so the obligation count 7,277 -> 7,299 is the new walks' guards and nothing else.

**What changes for a program.** A struct literal's braces after a failed expression are the expression's: the statements after it are the body's, and reported only for their own mistakes; a statement header that fails is its own report, and the body after it is read as the enclosing block's.

**Where it started.** DEF-279, registered at landing 145 from the DEF-269 agent's report: `int_form_and` (smt_encode.npk, D-279's Int forms of a mask) computed `m + 1i64` for a positive mask and `0i64 - m` for a negative one in the folder's `int64`, so the top mask `2^63 - 1` -- the fuzz corpus's `lx0332b`, `v & 9223372036854775807u64` -- trapped the compiler with IntOverflow under `--obligations`: exit 3 with no message, where the plain build compiles and runs the program. ### DEF-305 — LANDED 2026-10-24 (built, chained, recorded and landed by `nitpick-compiler_48` (Opus) in `wt/48f`, branch `48-def305` on landing 160's commit `954c21a`; landing 161)

A pointer that is a CALL'S RESULT has no named root (`place_root_symbol` 0), so `drop wp(raw idp(@h));` and `(raw idp(@h)).set()` reached neither DEF-302 reader; a pointer COMPOSED WITH A SLICE -- `bp.sl[0i64].o` (a slice field of a pointer's pointee) and `(<-tp)[0i64].o` (a pointer to a slice local) -- was asked only about the pointer's own chain (the address of the pointee), never the runs the pointee views.

**Measured first.** The four shapes on landing 160's compiler (`wt/48a/.internal/probes/m2/`, registered with DEF-302): `k302j` (the call-result argument), `k302k` (the call-result receiver), `v302a` (the pointer-and-slice field), `v302e` (the pointer to a slice local) -- 113/113 at both legs under landing 159's compiler and 160's; the three controls (a call-result write with no view live, a pointer-and-slice write with no view live, the view's block ended before the write) clean.

**The change.** Two mechanisms, both independent of M3. (A) escape.npk `escape_pointer_targets` (the twin of `escape_slice_targets`) resolves a pointer-valued expression -- a call's result or any pointer with no place chain -- by rule A (D-326): `collect_view_refs` reads what the result borrows, and each ref that is a TARGET of this pointer (a `REF_ADDR` of the pointee type, a `REF_PASS` of the pointer's type) is where it points. alias.npk `check_pointer_targets` gains a call-result arm (when `ptr_write_base` is none and the place is rootless and pointer-typed) that resolves by rule A, composes the whole write path, and checks both directions; `check_access_reported`'s rootless block routes a rootless pointer write/exclusive (not a `$$`-claim) there; `acc_call` drops the `is_place_chain` precondition on its pointer receiver and routes a pointer argument no place names through `check_written`. (B) escape.npk `escape_slice_targets` appends `escape_slice_targets_through_pointer`, which follows the pointer the slice base is reached through to its pointee and reads the pointee's chain for runs of the slice's element; `escape_slice_own_holder` resolves the single pointee so the slice's own view is skipped (no self-refusal), its validation walk stepping through a deref. AND `decreases fuel` on `pointer_targets_of` (alias.npk), discharging its stack-depth row (`open` at 160).

**The tests.** `tests/analysis/rejection/pointer_alias_adv.npk` (four sites, every one BORROW-015: the call-result argument (43:13) and receiver (49:11) at the call, the pointer-and-slice field (57:16) and the pointer-to-slice-local (65:17) at the write; three controls carry no code, in the ok file; its header generated by `header_from_check.py`; landing 160's checker reports none on it), `tests/backend/programs/pointer_alias_adv_ok.npk` (four checks, each failing at its own exit (11-14), exit 0 at both legs under this compiler and landing 160's; the four planted wrong expectations each fail at their own exit at both legs). No safe shape refused by design.

**The measurements**, all READ before the texts. THE SWEEP under landing 160's checker and this one over 5,282 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories, the fuzz corpus among them): 4 sites appeared (every one in the landing's own rejection file `pointer_alias_adv.npk`, `NITPICK-BORROW-015` -- the call-result argument (43:13), the call-result receiver (49:11), the pointer-and-slice field write (57:16) and the pointer-to-slice-local write (65:17); no file of `src/`, `npkg/`, `lib/`, `tools/`, the libraries, the applications or the fuzz corpus moves: nothing there writes through a call-result pointer under a live view of its pointee, or through a pointer to a slice under a view of the storage the slice spans), 0 vanished (none: a refusal added removes no site) -- 4,433 -> 4,437; per code NITPICK-BORROW-015 111 -> 115; the landing's whole refusal delta is the two new readers. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 612 programs under both compilers, 612 byte-identical, 0 different (none: the aliasing and escape analyses feed refusals alone, and the emitter is untouched), 0 newly refused, 0 (both compilers compile the new program test `pointer_alias_adv_ok.npk`, which holds the legal half, and the new rejection file is the sweep's) refused by the base alone. its `overflow` rows 5 -> 6), `alias.check_pointer_targets` (the call-result arm added: 37 lines, 1 removed, 19 -> 32 assertions, 1 -> 2 check-sat blocks; its `bounds` rows 1 -> 3) and `alias.pointer_targets_of` (the `decreases fuel`: 11 lines added, 0 removed, 231 -> 237 assertions, 21 -> 22 check-sat blocks; **What changes for a program.** A pointer that is a call's result targets the storage its return borrows, so a write through it -- handed to a writer, used as a writing receiver -- is a write to that storage; a pointer to a slice, or a slice field of a pointer's pointee, views the storage the slice spans. Holding a view of that storage by any name across such a write is BORROW-015. No safe shape refused by design, and no emission moves for a program that compiled before. DEF-305 fixed; DEF-306 registered; DEF-298, DEF-299, DEF-304 open.

### DEF-304 — LANDED 2026-10-26 (built, chained, recorded and landed by `nitpick-compiler_48` (Opus) in `wt/48g`, branch `48-def304` on landing 161's commit `249eae9`; landing 162)

**Where it started.** DEF-304 (registered at landing 160 measuring DEF-302's siblings; the author's Q75 answered option (a) FREEZE ≈19:55 2026-10-24 via `management_3`): a plain pointer into a `List` element held across an operation that reallocates, shrinks, reassigns, moves or drops the `List` read the FREED buffer at both legs on every compiler to date. `H2->:hp = @l[0i64];` then `list_push(@l, …)` past `cap` (the buffer grows), `list_reserve`, `list_truncate`, `list_pop`, `list_clear`, `l = …` (reassigned), `move(l)` into a dropping callee, or a `List` FIELD grown, then `hp.o.len` read -- the pointer reads where the freed buffer was. A VIEW of the element (`string_bytes(l[0i64].o)`, the twin k304a) was refused already (BORROW-015, D-325); the plain address was judged by NOTHING -- D-286's plain-address rule (`@` claims nothing, and the conflict table named claims and held `@`s, never a plain element address of managed storage that moves).

**Measured first.** The hazards and controls on landing 161's compiler (`wt/48a/.internal/probes/m2/u304*`, `results_u304_159.txt`): eight hazard shapes compiled and read the freed buffer at both legs -- `u304a` (the buffer grown, 11/11), `u304c` (reassigned, 11/11), `u304d` (truncated, 10/10), `u304e` (a field grown, 11/11), `u304f` (reserved, 11/11), `u304g` (popped, 10/10), `u304k` (moved out and dropped, 11/11), `u304l` (cleared, 10/10) -- while the two controls answered right: `u304h` (a pointer into a fixed ARRAY element across a reassignment, 0/0 -- an array's storage does not move) and `u304i` (a `$$i`/`$$m` claim held across a growth, already `NITPICK-BORROW-013`). The by-scope false positive `k304b` (the pointer dead before the growth but still in scope) and the two return/callee-provenance faces `u304j` (a call-result element pointer, 11/11) and `u304b` (an element pointer carried into a callee that reallocates through a pointer parameter, 11/11) are split off -- k304b accepted, u304j and u304b registered DEF-307.

**The change** (alias.npk, 13 edits; the plan `wt/48d/.internal/t160/DEF304_PLAN.md`). A new party kind `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` (= 5, after `PARTY_LENT` = 4), raised by `party_kind_of` where an `@place` expression (`ExprAddressOfExpr`) addresses a `List` element: `place_is_list_element` walks the addressed place (a `BORROW_FUEL` loop, measured) and answers true at an index whose base is a `List` (`type_is_list` on the base's type, `use "../type_layout.npk".*;` added for it), descending through `MemberAccess`, `SafeNav` and `Deref`, and answers false for a fixed array or a slice (whose storage does not move). The kind is VIEW-LIKE: `conflicts` places it in the VIEW/SHARED/LENT group (conflicting with `WRITE|EXCLUSIVE`), `summary_judged` includes it, and `check_access_reported` routes it -- before the `PARTY_VIEW` arm -- to `report_elem_addr_conflict` (modeled on `report_view_conflict`, code `NITPICK-BORROW-015`, a tailored message). So it is judged by the MUTATION-SUMMARY path (`check_written` -> `CHECK_VIEWS`) exactly as the view party is: a reallocating operation on `l` is a write of `l`'s storage, and the live `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` rooted at `l` conflicts with it. `party_word` gains its arm ("the plain address into a `List` element"). Five `PARTY_PLAIN` sites (the non-local assign, the named-root `admit_value`, the `acc_call` claim loop, the ordinary-arg loop and the final claim recheck) guard `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` like `PLAIN`; `prescan_party` pushes it with `root_ptr:false` and `register_holder` makes it live for all kinds, so the pointer-exemption (the `root_ptr` test) never applies.

THE DESIGN REFINEMENT (recorded against the plan). The plan sketched `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` as a STANDALONE kind, adding `ACC_SHARED` and relying on a blanket `admit_written` of `ACC_WRITE`. The build made it VIEW-LIKE and `summary_judged` instead -- more precise: it never fires on `@l` handed to a READ-ONLY callee (the callee's mutation summary carries no write of `l`), it does not conflict with a `$$i`/`$$m` claim where a view would not (the claims keep D-286's same-root rule), and the claim-held hazard `u304i` stays caught by the claim's OWN party (`NITPICK-BORROW-013`). The `place_is_list_element` walk is fuel-bounded with a measure (D-304).

**The tests.** `tests/analysis/rejection/list_elem_ptr.npk` (nine sites, every one `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the operation: `grow_push` (58:20), `reserve_op` (66:23), `truncate_op` (75:24), `pop_op` (84:26), `clear_op` (92:21), `reassign_op` (100:5), `field_grow` (108:20), `move_out` (117:19), and `unused_after_growth` (125:20) -- k304b, the ACCEPTED by-scope false positive, the pointer dead before the growth but still in scope; its header generated by `header_from_check.py` to a fixed point of nine; landing 161's checker reports none on it), `tests/backend/programs/list_elem_ptr_ok.npk` (three legal controls: `scope_ended` -- a pointer whose block ENDS before the op, reads 2; `by_index` -- a read by INDEX after a growth with no pointer held, 2; `array_elem` -- a pointer into a fixed ARRAY element across a reassignment, 4, u304h, arrays never reallocate; exit 0 at both legs under this compiler AND landing 161's; the three planted wrong expectations each fail at their own exit, 11-13). The eight hazard spellings refused, the controls clean. The overlap works because `l[i]` canonicalises to `l.items[…]` (D-314 §2), so the `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` at `@l[i]` meets the reallocating op's write of `l.items`.

**The measurements**, all READ before the texts. THE SWEEP under landing 161's checker and this one over 5,284 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories, the fuzz corpus among them): 9 sites appeared (every one in the landing's own rejection file `list_elem_ptr.npk`, `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the operation -- `grow_push` (58:20), `reserve_op` (66:23), `truncate_op` (75:24), `pop_op` (84:26), `clear_op` (92:21), `reassign_op` (100:5), `field_grow` (108:20), `move_out` (117:19) and the accepted by-scope false positive `unused_after_growth` (125:20, k304b); no file of `src/`, `npkg/`, `lib/`, `tools/`, the libraries, the applications or the fuzz corpus moves: nothing there holds a plain pointer into a `List` element across a reallocating or dropping operation), 0 vanished (none: a refusal added removes no site) -- 4,437 -> 4,446; per code NITPICK-BORROW-015 115 -> 124; the landing's whole refusal delta is the one new party kind. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 613 programs under both compilers, 613 byte-identical, 0 different (none: the aliasing walk feeds refusals alone, and the emitter is untouched), 0 newly refused, 0 (both compilers compile the new program test `list_elem_ptr_ok.npk`, which holds the legal half, and the new rejection file is the sweep's) refused by the base alone. The libraries and applications: 4,064 programs, 1,892 byte-identical, 2,172 refused by both, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by the base alone; the tree's and the libraries' differing pairs 0, so `tidnorm`/`dropnorm` ran over none. D-332's count and D-237's set over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files (set-check 316 files). The program suite by hand: 422 programs at both legs, 413 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The verify suite: 138 files PASS; the problem text of 138 of 138 files byte-identical under both compilers. The frontend's 39 and the emitter's 6 unit tests pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree -- 7,563 obligations (3,633 discharged, 2,939 open, 0 budget, 986 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations` 7,264 rows before and 7,264 after, 7,150 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell; the 114 rows not shared on each side are 114 RE-KEYED, 0 ADDED and 0 REMOVED -- an id move: the landing's three new interned symbols (`PARTY_ELEM_ADDR`, `place_is_list_element`, `report_elem_addr_conflict`) shift the interned-id-based keys (D-317) of 114 rows in other functions; read with the row-key dropped and every id blanked, the base and new manifests are an IDENTICAL multiset (base-only 0, new-only 0), the (kind, verdict) multiset identical both ways; the landing's new functions carry NO obligation row (`place_is_list_element` a counted loop with a boolean/enum body, `report_elem_addr_conflict` a string built through calls -- neither generates one) and the edited functions' rows are byte-identical (`check_access_reported`'s one `bounds`/`unencoded` row the same hash; `party_kind_of`, `conflicts`, `party_word` and the five guarded `PARTY_PLAIN` sites add only boolean predicates); the base dump regenerated from landing 161's compiler matches 249eae9's committed manifest 7,264 of 7,264, and this compiler's dump matches the recorded manifest 7,264 of 7,264; so the verdict totals stand (checker 5, discharged 3,342, open 2,931, unencoded 986) and the obligation count is unmoved (7,563 -> 7,563); the floor's 388 unmoved. The in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A plain pointer into a `List` element now freezes the `List` against any operation that could move or free its backing buffer, for the pointer's lexical lifetime, exactly as a view of the element does (D-325) -- `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the operation. Hold the index and re-read `l[i]`, or end the pointer's block before the operation. A pointer into a fixed ARRAY element is untouched (an array's storage does not move). No emission moves for a program that compiled before. DEF-304 fixed (option (a) FREEZE, the author's Q75); DEF-307 registered (the call-result and pointer-parameter element-address faces); DEF-298, DEF-299, DEF-306 open.

### DEF-306 + DEF-308 — LANDED 2026-10-26 (built, chained, recorded and landed by `nitpick-compiler_49` (Opus) in `wt/49c`, branch `49-def306` on landing 162's commit `c79a2c0`; landing 163)

**Where it started.** DEF-306 (registered at landing 161 by `nitpick-compiler_48`, the probe s302s2; HIGH): a spawn LENDS its crossing until the block's join (`NITPICK-BORROW-016`, D-180), tracked by the ROOT symbol handed to the spawn, but a reassignment or drop of the crossing reached by a POINTER under another name was not resolved to the crossing's root -- `shared_arena<int64>:s = shared_arena_make(8i64); shared_arena<int64>->:sp = @s; drop filler(@s); drop replace(sp);` (`replace` doing `(<-p) = shared_arena_make(4i64)`) dropped the old arena under the live `filler` task: a use-after-free / data race in safe code at both legs, on every compiler to date. DEF-308 is the VIEW twin, folded here: DEF-302 (landing 160) closed a DIRECT whole-pointee write through a named pointer against a view by another name, but a CALLEE that replaces the whole pointee (`wr(hp)`, `(<-p) = …`), handed a named pointer, under a view `string_bytes(h.o)` by the pointee's own name, read 113 at both legs. Both are the lend and the view faces of DEF-302/DEF-305's name-vs-pointer gap.

**Measured first.** The class was mapped by a MATRIX over the axes holder (local, copy, local-field, two-level-field, second-ptr, call-result) × side (forward, reverse) × write (assign, destroy, move, replace-callee) × pointer-path, each cell a probe run at both legs (`wt/49a/.internal/probes/def306/`, `gen_def306.py`, `gen_def306b.py`, `matrix306.py`). Under landing 162's compiler the matrix exits 1 (70 un-listed open cells, the live exit-(i) control; `NPKX_NOLEGS` skips the repro legs). Under the fix it exits 0: 405 cells -- 158 REFUSED, 5 KNOWN-LIMIT citing DEF-309/DEF-310, 0 OPEN/UNEXPECTED/STALE, 242 N/A; 13/13 controls; the self-test proves both fail-closed exits (a non-listed open cell, and a known-limit cell turned REFUSED). The five open cells are silent on landing 162 too (pre-existing, not regressions). Kept as the class's regression tool at `.internal/def306_matrix_kept/` (18 files + README: after DEF-309/310 the five flip to REFUSED and the matrix FAILS -- the signal to empty KNOWN_LIMIT).

**The change** (alias.npk). `ptr_place_written_whole` answers whether a place is a NAMED pointer place written WHOLE by `ap` (pointer-typed, rooted at a name, `ap` its own steps and nothing below). `check_pointer_targets` gains a whole-pointee route: under `CHECK_VIEWS`/`CHECK_LENT`, such a write routes to `pe = place`, the pointer re-rooted at its target (DEF-302's `pointer_targets_of`, DEF-305's `escape_pointer_targets`, `ptr_last_base`) and checked against the VIEW and LENT parties there (`check_view_parties_at`, which now reads both). `lend_through_pointer` (in `lend_spawn_args`) resolves a spawn's pointer argument -- an `@place` by `ptr_last_base`+`pointer_targets_of`, a named pointer by `pointer_targets_of`, a call's result by `escape_pointer_targets` (D-326 rule A) -- and raises a LENT party per target beside the party rooted at the pointer itself; the spawned callee is read against the lend for what it writes through a pointer VALUE. A pointer PARAMETER has no target here (its pointee is the caller's; its lend the pointer's).

**Management's ruling** (B with conditions, 2026-10-26, via `management_3`). Close the class via the matrix; register the five open cells as two HIGH defects KNOWN-LIMIT by EXACT cell id (never pattern), the check failing closed on any OTHER open cell AND if a known-limit cell turns REFUSED; a control for each exit; keep the matrix as a tool; schedule DEF-309 and DEF-310 FIRST next week before M3, each measured before fixed, in STATE's handoff. DEF-309 (direct whole-pointee write/move through a CALL-RESULT pointer, four cells `m_{lend,view}_forward_{assign,move}_call_result`; cause: `check_access_reported`'s root==0 call-result arm keys on the written place being pointer-typed, but a direct deref write's place is the pointee, the rootless pointer one level up -- a deref-of-rootless-pointer arm). DEF-310 (`destroy` through a pointer FIELD escapes `NITPICK-TYPE-091`, one cell `m_lend_forward_destroy_local_field`; `sp.destroy()` and `(raw idp(@s)).destroy()` are TYPE-091, `pa.p.destroy()` compiles). Two over-refusals accepted: c306f/c308f, a re-pointed pointer written under a lend/view of its first target (flow-insensitive `pointer_targets_of`, the twin of k302a, D-286). N/A one-liners kept: a `Mutex` has no `destroy` (D-056/TYPE-007), a view through a call-result pointer is BORROW-012.

**The scope grep.** A scope-exclusion grep over `src/` and `tools/` (every "scope"/"Until this landing"/deferral hit): clean -- DEF-305's "outside this defect's scope" comment rewritten to cite DEF-306/DEF-308; remaining hits are stage boundaries, historical notes, or deferrals that cite D-286 or describe current behaviour.

**The tests.** `tests/analysis/rejection/spawn_lent_through_pointer.npk` (thirteen sites, every one `NITPICK-BORROW-016`: the reverse -- a lend through a pointer, the crossing written by its own name (88:5, 97:5, 106:34, 116:18), a copy (126:5), a local field (135:5), a two-level field (145:5), a call-result pointer handed to the spawn (153:5), a `Mutex` moved by name (162:33); the forward -- a lend by name, the whole pointee written through a pointer (173:18, 183:18); the task itself storing over its crossing through a pointer local (193:13); and the accepted over-refusal (209:18, c306f)), `tests/analysis/rejection/view_whole_pointee.npk` (six sites, every one `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the call: a named pointer (47:13), a copy (58:13), a local field (68:15), a two-level field (79:16), a second pointer (90:13), and the accepted over-refusal (107:13, c308f)), `tests/backend/programs/view_whole_pointee_ok.npk` (three controls, exits 11-13), `tests/backend/programs/spawn_lent_through_pointer_ok.npk` (`// stress: 40`, `// explore: 1000`, three controls over a `Channel`, exits 11-15); each header generated by `header_from_check.py`, landing 162's checker reporting none on either rejection file; the planted wrong expectations each fail at their own exit at both legs (`.internal/planted163.sh`).

**The measurements**, all READ before the texts. THE SWEEP under landing 162's checker and this one over 5,288 files: 19 sites appeared (every one in the landing's own two rejection files -- 13 BORROW-016, 6 BORROW-015), 0 vanished -- 4,446 → 4,465; per code BORROW-015 124 → 130, BORROW-016 10 → 23; no file of `src/`, `npkg/`, `lib/`, `tools/`, the libraries, the applications or the fuzz corpus moves. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 615 tree programs byte-identical under both compilers, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 base-only; the libraries and applications 4,064 programs, 1,892 byte-identical, 2,172 refused by both, 0 different. D-332's count and D-237's set over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files (set-check 318 files). The verify suite 138 PASS, 138/138 problem texts byte-identical; the frontend's 39 and the emitter's 6 unit tests pass. THE MANIFEST: 7,565 obligations (3,634 discharged, 2,939 open, 0 budget, 987 unencoded, 5 checker), the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 unmoved; `nitpick.obligations` 7,264 → 7,266 rows, 7,170 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, ZERO discharged counts fell; 94 RE-KEYED (the two new symbols' id move, 28 functions of 6 modules), 2 ADDED (`lend_spawn_args` 1 overflow discharged, `lend_through_pointer` 1 bounds unencoded), 0 REMOVED; the in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A spawn's crossing is now frozen against a write reached through a pointer by any name until the block's join, and a view of a pointee is frozen against a whole-pointee-replacing callee handed a pointer -- two use-after-frees / data races in safe code closed. Nothing legal is refused except a re-pointed pointer written under a lend or view of its first target (store a copy, or write before re-pointing). ### DEF-309 + DEF-310 + DEF-311 — LANDED 2026-10-28 (built, chained, recorded and landed by `nitpick-compiler_51` (Fable) in `wt/51a`, branch `51-def309` on landing 163's commit `1480ca8`; landing 164)

DEF-311 was found measuring them (below) and folded in by management at 21:09.

**The change.** (alias.npk) `rootless_deref_base` walks a written place inward over its members and elements to the first EXPLICIT dereference and answers its operand when that is pointer-typed with no named root; `path_steps(x, place, stop, out)` gained the `stop` (eight callers pass none); `check_access_reported`'s root==0 block, where DEF-305's arm does not fire, hands that pointer to `check_pointer_targets`' call-result arm (rule A, D-326) with the steps between composed onto each target ahead of what the callee writes -- `(<-(raw idb(@hb))).k` stays disjoint from a view of `hb.o`; `acc_place`'s non-chain branch checks such a WRITE or EXCLUSIVE place once (`check_access_ex`, CHECK_ALL) and then walks the steps down to the dereference, handing the dereference to `acc_expr`, so DEF-123's shared-holder refusal stays and no site reports twice (before this edit `(<-(raw idarr(@arr)))[0i64].o` reported twice). `alias_decl` sets `x.esc.cur_fn` to the function it walks around `alias_fn` and restores it (DEF-311): rule A's readers recognise a parameter only through `symbol_is_param` -> `param_decl_index` -> `cur_fn`, which `escape_decl` restores to -1 after each function; stale, a pointer parameter read as a `for` binding with no chain. (type_members.npk) `destroy_receiver_owned` tests EVERY node on the receiver's path for its own type -- a pointer, a slice or a handle anywhere on it is TYPE-091 (DEF-310). `NITPICK-BORROW-015`/`-016` at the write, the move, the call or the spawn; `NITPICK-TYPE-091` at the call.

**The change** (alias.npk alone). THE PARTY IS RAISED for every way an element address is obtained (DEF-307): `prescan_view_parties` reads the holder's value type off its declaration's initialiser and, for each `REF_ADDR` among the holder's recorded refs, raises `PARTY_ELEM_ADDR` when the addressed place is a `List` element (`place_is_list_element`), when a POINTER holder's ref addresses a whole `List` whose type is not the holder's pointee (`H2->:e = raw first(@l)`, `string->:s = raw firsto(@l)`: rule A, D-326 -- the result points INTO the `List`; the party's place is the `List`), or when an AGGREGATE holder's ref addresses a `List` and the holder's type holds a pointer whose pointee is not the `List` (`type_holds_elem_pointer`, `decreases fuel`, fuel 8: a pointer member whose pointee is not the `List`, `dyn`, `any`, an enum with payloads, an unresolved type or fuel run out answer yes -- DEF-54's closed direction; a struct, `Optional`, `Result`, array, slice or `List` when a member does; a scalar or a string never; a pointer TO the `List` is D-286's plain party); `prescan_party` pushes one party per address (`pre_has_elem_party`) and marks a pointer root; `acc_call`'s claim loop pushes a party of the `List` FOR THE CALL for an element-address argument (holder 0, truncated at the call's end), judged against the other arguments' summaries; `check_access_reported`'s same-root loop skips a party at the address-taking that made it and a call's transient party at its own argument; `party_word` says which. THE PARTY IS JUDGED by the pointer readers (DEF-314): `check_view_parties_at` admits the kind beside VIEW and LENT and gains `own`, the bare pointer binding the write goes through (`check_pointer_targets` passes it for a named pointer, 0 for a call result) -- a write through the address's own holder, or strictly below the elements (`write_into_elements` at the party's `elem_party_level`: into a PART of an element, the summary's `items/x/f…`), is exempt, every other write reaches `report_elem_addr_conflict`; `check_pointer_view_parties` admits it too, the party re-rooted at its pointer's target as a view is, its `List` level carried across. `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the operation.

**The change** (alias.npk and escape.npk; the emitter untouched). DEF-315: `acc_call`'s receiver branch hands the bare dereference whose operand `rootless_deref_base` answers to `check_written` as the implicit `@recv` of a `Self->` method with a summary — DEF-309's road: the root==0 block, `rootless_deref_base`, `check_pointer_targets`' call-result arm (a parameter root by DEF-311, two pointees, the LENT parties with the views); a member or an index below the dereference stays `acc_place`'s whole-place check. DEF-316: `check_access_reported`'s root==0 block runs the named path's slice dispatch after its pointer arms — `escape_written_slice_base` finds the slice below the dereference, `check_slice_targets` judges the write at the storage the slice views, the targets by DEF-305 (b)'s through-pointer reader (`escape_first_pointer_inward`, factored out of it and shared by three readers, finds the call-result pointer on the base's path; the pointee's chain is read) and the own holder by `escape_slice_own_holder_rootless` (i): the SINGLE pointee of that pointer, a slice or a one-view struct. DEF-317: `escape_slice_targets` follows every ref of the base that is not a run ITSELF (`escape_ref_is_strict_run`: the end type an array, a slice or a `List` of the element, against the MAY of `escape_ref_is_run_of`, which answers yes for a carrier), or that is a run reached THROUGH a pointer, into its root's chain — `escape_carrier_runs(root, pte, fuel, seen, out)`, `decreases fuel`: each ref of the root that may be a run is a target, each that is a carrier is followed with one unit of fuel less, a root already seen adds nothing and spends none; four units, so three pointer LINKS resolve (`c3 -> c2 -> c1 -> b`); and it answers whether EVERY chain was read. The own holder of a call-result slice is its carrier ((ii): the ONE root among the call's refs, each pointer-typed ref resolved to its single pointee first, so `getsl(bp)`'s `{bp, b}` is one root; two pointees or two roots skip none); the walk down to the call steps `raw`, `?!` (`ExprEmphaticUnwrapExpr`: the value in slot a, the trap code in slot b — NOT `ExprRelayExpr`, the `relay` keyword's node), `??!` (`ExprEmphaticCoalesceExpr`), `relay`, `await` and the casts — the operators that are the call's value on ONE path; `?|` and `??` are two values and are not stepped (no own holder, the closed direction). THE FUEL FAILS CLOSED (management_4, 03:06): a chain still to follow at fuel 0 leaves the slice UNRESOLVED and the write is refused, `NITPICK-BORROW-009` at the slice (`unresolved_slice_text`: "a write through a slice whose storage this analysis cannot resolve … hold the carrier in a local and take the slice from it"), where the escape pass records the write (`record_mut_of_slice_targets`, `record_refs_into_unnamed_slice` -> `escape_unresolved_slice`: once per node, the node kept in `x.unresolved` on the `Escape` state, in the reporting round) and in `check_slice_targets` for a node the escape pass did not reach — one report per site, never "no targets". The review's P4 beside it: `type_holds_elem_pointer` answers YES for `TY_PARAM`, `TY_SELF`, `TY_ASSOC` and `TY_INVALID`, the closed direction its comment and the docs claimed. `NITPICK-BORROW-015` at the method, the write, the move or the call; `NITPICK-BORROW-016` under a lend; `NITPICK-BORROW-009` past the fuel. THE TWO SELF-REFUSALS ON THE WAY: the first build's own-holder walk stopped at the `raw` node over the call and five controls refused against their own carrier's view (c10, c11, c12, c17n, c18n; the fix of 04:12 steps the unwrap operators and the casts, management_6's guard a hazard cell and an own-view control per kind, `cells4/`); with those named it still stopped at `?!`, whose node kind is `ExprEmphaticUnwrapExpr`, so t1n and t3n refused against the carrier's own view and t1 and t3 for the WRONG view (the carrier's, not `vw`'s) — found by a diagnostic build in `wt/51f` that gave each `pass 0i32` of the two own-holder readers its own negative code and printed it through `alias_err` (exit 3: the stop-walk's "neither a call nor a stepped kind"); the fix of 05:00 names `ExprEmphaticUnwrapExpr` and `ExprEmphaticCoalesceExpr` in `escape_first_pointer_inward` and both walks of `escape_slice_own_holder_rootless` (+7 lines; `cells4_reason.sh` reads every slice-road refusal naming `vw`'s view). **What changes for a program.** A `Self->` method on the bare dereference of a call-result pointer, a write through a slice reached below such a dereference, and a call-result slice handed out of a struct carrier — behind a call-result or a named pointer, a pointer field, up to three pointer-field links, or the carrier's slice field copied by name, the result wrapped in `raw`, `?!`, `??!`, `relay`, `await` or a cast — are judged at the storage they reach, against its views and its lends: three use-after-frees in safe code closed (fifty-four of 127 measured cells silent on 165 refused now, thirty-four of them reading the freed bytes at both legs). A carrier chain not resolved within three pointer links — the fourth link — is refused outright (`NITPICK-BORROW-009`: hold the carrier in a local and take the slice from it). No safe single-carrier shape is refused; eleven over-refusals are accepted, each the named road's own verdict on the same storage or the fuel's closed direction (0 tree sites each). Registered open: DEF-324 (HIGH, landing 167 with DEF-312 widened; first if 167 splits) and DEF-325 (LOW) from the condition cells; DEF-318 to DEF-323 (HIGH, landing 168) from management's review of 165.

**What changes for a program.** A view or a slice of a local returned through an explicit dereference is refused at the frame exit as the auto-dereference's always was; a view stored or handed on through a dereference into a parameter's pointee from a block-local carrier is refused at the call; a write — direct, by move, by call or by method — through a pointer copied through a dereference, through a slice copied from a carrier, at a two-view holder's field address, through a field-address getter's result, through a pointer cast to or laundered through `any->`, through a cast or a local copy of a pointer parameter inside a callee, or through a cast dereference, under a view or a claim of the storage it reaches, is refused where the same shape by name is; a plain address cast as it is taken is a plain party beside a claim; a write whose steps the aliasing walk cannot spell is refused rather than admitted; and a `=>!` or `=>` between two plain pointer types with unrelated pointees is refused at the cast in safe code — the same pointee, `any->` and the wild crossings stay. Nothing a program that compiled before does is emitted differently; the SIX accepted over-refusals have 0 tree sites -- five parity-matched, and the sixth (r4, build 15's and 16's) the WHOLE reading of a write through a pointee-changing cast, which cannot know a round trip is round.

DEF-345 (OPEN HIGH, numbered by management_6 at 11:53:32 from nitpick-libs_29's report of 11:34:37 — a library worker's reading of bindings.npk, the accept test and ir_stmt.npk: `$$m x` of a binding declared without an initialiser ASSIGNS it to the definite-assignment analysis (D-118), nothing binds the callee to write its `$$m` parameter, and D-225's vacant value is written for a dropping type alone — so a non-dropping local reads the previous frame's bytes. **The change** (alias.npk, escape.npk; the emitter untouched; `t168/build168/src168.diff` reproduces the two files from the base's byte for byte -- verified by sha256 against a scratch checkout --, `t167/build167/apply_src168.py` the first build's anchored form). ALIAS: `prescan_expr_picks` is walked from EVERY expression slot `alias_stmt` reads -- the initialiser, the assigned value and its TARGET, the operand of `pass`, `fail`, `return` and `exit`, an `if`'s, a `while`'s and a `when`'s condition, a `for`'s iterable, a counted `loop`'s and `till`'s arguments, a statement pick's selector, an arm's `where` guard, the generic `stmt_carries_expr` slot -- and enters a pick expression's arms as `prescan_stmt` enters a statement pick's (`StmtPickArm`), so the arm's holders are registered before the aliasing walk reaches them; it steps the CUSTOM shapes through `prescan_pick_window(x, start, count, depth)` -- a call's callee and its VALUE arguments after the generic ones (`expr_callee`, `expr_generic_count`), a builtin's after `builtin_generic_count`, a struct literal's values at the odd slots of its NAME/VALUE window -- and fails CLOSED on a missing or unknown shape (`alias_err(BORROW_INTERNAL, …)`, as `acc_expr` fails); `acc_expr`'s Drop arm and `acc_call` admit a METHOD spawn, its arguments lent as a free function's (the receiver is judged by the escape pass's rule, never lent). ESCAPE: `escape_stmt`'s `for` arm walks the iterable ALWAYS (it walked it only under `for_binding_can_carry`, a type question, so a pick in a range bound was never entered and its arm's holder had no view ref), the untracked-borrow refusal unchanged under the guard; `collect_gives_refs` and `collect_gives_roots` enter an `if`'s then-branch by `stmt_then` and every loop kind's body -- `while`, `for`, the counted `loop`, `till`, and a `when`'s body, `then` and `end` -- the statement shapes a `give` can stand under (the escape walk's own, minus `defer`: TYPE-094); `callee_is_async(x, call)` reads the RECORDED callee first (`call_callee_fn`, the call graph's edge, which names a method, a module- or type-qualified spelling and an identifier alike) and keeps the identifier read as the fallback; both spawn arms admit `ExprMethodCallExpr`; the receiver is handed to `escape_spawn_recv` as the implicit first argument unless the callee is module-qualified (`exprtypes_ns_decl != 0`: no receiver, position base 0) -- a POINTER receiver is `NITPICK-BORROW-004` as `drop fillw(@w, @s)` is (D-180: no Mutex, RwLock, CondVar, Barrier or shared_arena takes a user method, TYPE-007, so the receiver is a plain struct's; an unknown callee, a `dyn` call, is read as a pointer receiver, the closed direction), a BY-VALUE receiver goes to DEF-340's rule; the arguments go through `escape_spawn_args(x, call, pos_base, start, count, depth)`, and `escape_spawn_byval` refuses a by-value argument or receiver whose type DROPS (`type_drops`) unless the callee's parameter at that position is declared `move` (`spawn_param_is_move`, read off the recorded callee's declaration: the function TYPE carries no qualifier) -- a `Channel` crosses by value (D-181) and plain data copies harmlessly; the message names the remedies (`move(...)` into a `move` parameter; `.clone()` first; a sanctioned pointer). THE SECOND BUILD (7891a43, the same day): the first build's `prescan_expr_picks` dispatched on `expr_shape` alone and bailed on every CUSTOM shape (`ExprCallExpr`, `ExprBuiltinExpr`, `ExprStructLiteralExpr`, `ExprPickExpr`), so a pick in a CALL ARGUMENT (p4) and in a STRUCT LITERAL's field (p8) stayed silent under it (113/113 and 5/5 at both legs) while the nine other slots moved; the walker steps the four custom kinds as `acc_expr` steps them, and CUSTOM and NONE are an INTERNAL refusal -- the closed direction; the cells' second run moved both (`cells168_168a.txt` the first build's verdicts, `cells168_168b.txt` the second's). THE THIRD BUILD (the same day; management_6 18:34: accepted as one landing at +197/−18 code lines, past +210 a split): probing management's 18:07 ask -- every cell named AND inline wherever a spelling can differ -- found `prescan_stmt` walking only the BODY slots of six statement kinds, so a pick expression in a `for` iterable's range bound (q1, q1b), a counted `loop`'s argument (q2), a `when` condition (q3), an assignment TARGET's index (q5), an arm's `where` guard (q6) or a statement pick's or a pick expression's selector with a PLAIN give (q7, q8; q4, a selector GIVING the view, `-009` already) kept its arm's holders unregistered under the second build as under 167; and behind q1 a second gap in the ESCAPE pass: `escape_stmt`'s `for` arm walked the iterable only under `if (for_binding_can_carry(…))`, so a pick in a range bound was never entered and its arm's holder had no view ref at all -- q1 silent on 166, on 167 and on the second build alike (113/113 at each). Fixed by the six slots in `prescan_stmt` (`stmt_target`, a `when`'s `stmt_cond`, `stmt_for_iter`, each `stmt_counted_arg`, `pick_selector`, `arm_guard`) and the unconditional walk in the `for` arm. The mechanical check is THE SLOT TABLE of `meta/roadmap/1.6/tools/kindtable167.py` (extended here, `--out PATH`): per statement walker (`escape_stmt`, `alias_stmt`, `prescan_stmt`) and statement kind, each expression accessor's value `walked (entries)`, `walked (entry) under if (cond)` or `READ` only -- at 49754e4 `escape_stmt / StmtForStmt: stmt_for_iter: walked (escape_untracked) under if (raw for_binding_can_carry(x, fit))` and `prescan_stmt`'s cond, iterable, counted arguments, selector, guard and target READ or absent; at this landing every slot of the three walkers is walked unguarded (`t168/kindtable167_at_16.md`, `t168/kindtable168.md`). **What changes for a program.** REFUSALS ADDED: an owning `move` receiver that is a place, an operator over one, a part of a temporary or reached through a pointer (`NITPICK-TYPE-046` at the receiver; `(move(x)).m()`, `(move(<-p)).m()`); an owner copied through a ternary, `??`, `??!`, `?!`, `?|`, `relay`, a cast or `=> dyn`, or a part read through `?.`, at any move slot (TYPE-046; `move(...)` at the place inside the operator); an owning part of a value no binding holds at a move slot (TYPE-046; bind first). A REFUSAL KEPT: a spawned by-value receiver through a pointer whose pointee owns or holds an address (`NITPICK-BORROW-004`; refused on 169 whatever the pointee). ONE KIND REMOVED: the same spawn over PLAIN DATA, which reads the right value now. EMISSION MOVED only where a by-value method is awaited or spawned through a pointer -- the pointee loaded into the child's frame --, which no program of the tree or the libraries that compiled before does.
